footnotetext:
Sections marked with a * symbol provide background reviews. Calculations supporting the results in the main text are given in sections without the * symbol.

Appendix A
Local interaction

A.1
Local (moiré) orbitals *

Here we summarize the relevant local interactions in a single AA-stacking region of magic-angle twisted bilayer graphene (MATBG).
There are eight effective local
f
-orbitals in an AA-stacking region
Song and Bernevig 2022
:
f_{\alpha,\eta,s}
, where
\alpha=1,2
is the orbital index,
\eta=\pm
is the valley index, and
s=\uparrow,\downarrow
is the spin index.
A single AA-site has the time-reversal symmetry (
T
) and symmetries of the
D_{6}
point group.
The single-particle representations of these discrete symmetries are given in section S2A of the supplementary material of Ref.
Song and Bernevig 2022
.
They are

T=\sigma^{0}\tau^{x}\varsigma^{0}K,\qquad C_{2z}=\sigma^{x}\tau^{x}\varsigma^{0},\qquad C_{3z}=e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{0}},\qquad C_{2x}=\sigma^{x}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{0}\ ,

(5)

where
K
is the complex conjugation.
Their actions on the second quantized operators can be obtained directly

Tf_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}T^{-1}=f^{\dagger}_{\alpha\overline{\eta}s},\qquad C_{2z}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}C_{2z}^{-1}=f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger},\qquad C_{3z}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}C_{3z}^{-1}=e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\eta(-1)^{\alpha-1}}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger},\qquad C_{2x}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}C_{2x}^{-1}=f^{\dagger}_{\overline{\alpha}\eta s}\ .

(6)

In this work we
do not
distinguish the single-particle and the second-quantized representations of symmetry operators.
If we write a unitary (anti-unitary) symmetry operator
g
as a matrix
D^{g}
(
D^{g}\cdot K
), as exampled in
eq.

5
, then its action on second-quantized operators is defined by

gf^{\dagger}_{\alpha\eta s}g^{-1}=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}D_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime},\alpha\eta s}^{g}f_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger},\qquad gf_{\alpha\eta s}g^{-1}=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}D_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime},\alpha\eta s}^{g*}f_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\ .

(7)

We denote the fermion creation operator of the
p_{z}
orbital at the carbon atom belonging to the layer
l
(=
+
for the top layer and
-
for the bottom layer) by
\psi_{l\mathbf{r}s}^{\dagger}
.
Here
s
is the spin index,
\mathbf{r}\in\mathcal{L}_{l,1}+\mathcal{L}_{l,2}
is the position of the atom, and
\mathcal{L}_{l,\beta}
is the
\beta
-sub-lattice of the layer
l
, where we associate the A-, B-sub-lattices of graphene to
\beta=1
and 2, respectively.
Projected
\psi_{l\mathbf{r}s}^{\dagger}
can be written in terms of the
f
operators as

\psi_{l\mathbf{r}s}^{\dagger}=\sqrt{\Omega_{\rm G}}\sum_{\eta\alpha}\sum_{\beta,\mathcal{L}_{l,\beta}\ni\mathbf{r}}e^{-i\eta\mathbf{K}_{l}\cdot\mathbf{r}}w_{l\beta,\alpha}^{(\eta)*}(\mathbf{r})f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}\ .

(8)

Here
\pm\mathbf{K}_{l}
are the momenta of Dirac points in the layer
l
,

\mathbf{K}_{+}=\frac{4\pi}{3a_{\rm G}}(\cos\frac{\theta}{2},\sin\frac{\theta}{2})
,

\mathbf{K}_{-}=\frac{4\pi}{3a_{\rm G}}(\cos\frac{\theta}{2},-\sin\frac{\theta}{2})
,
with
a_{\rm G}=2.46\mathring{\rm A}
being the graphene lattice constant and
\theta\approx 1.08^{\circ}
the magic twist angle.
The length of
\mathbf{K}_{l}
is given by
|\mathbf{K}_{l}|=\frac{4\pi}{3a_{\rm G}}\approx 1.703\mathring{\rm A}^{-1}
.

\Omega_{\rm G}=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}a_{\rm G}^{2}
is the area of a graphene unit cell.

w_{l\beta,\alpha}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{r})
is the localized Wannier functions constructed in Ref.
Song and Bernevig 2022
.
The summation over the sub-lattice index
\beta
on the right hand side is limited to the one containing
\mathbf{r}
on the left hand side,
i.e.
,
\mathcal{L}_{l,\beta}\ni\mathbf{r}
.
Section S2A of the supplementary material of Ref.
Song and Bernevig 2022
provides a Gaussian approximation for the Wannier functions:

w_{l1,1}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{r})=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi\lambda_{1}^{2}}}e^{i\frac{\pi}{4}l\eta-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda_{1}^{2})},\quad w_{l2,1}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{r})=-l\frac{\alpha_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{x+i\eta y}{\lambda_{2}^{2}\sqrt{\pi}}e^{i\frac{\pi}{4}l\eta-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda_{2}^{2})},

(9)

w_{l1,2}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{r})=l\frac{\alpha_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{x-i\eta y}{\lambda_{2}^{2}\sqrt{\pi}}e^{-i\frac{\pi}{4}l\eta-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda_{2}^{2})},\qquad w_{l2,2}^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{r})=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi\lambda_{1}^{2}}}e^{-i\frac{\pi}{4}l\eta-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda_{1}^{2})}\ .

(10)

At the magic-angle, the parameters are estimated as

\alpha_{1}=0.8193,\qquad\alpha_{2}=0.5734,\qquad\lambda_{1}=0.1791a_{\rm M},\qquad\lambda_{2}=0.1910a_{\rm M}\ .

(11)

where
a_{\rm M}=\frac{4\pi}{3k_{\theta}}=\frac{a_{\rm G}}{2\sin\frac{\theta}{2}}\approx 54.57a_{\rm G}\approx 134.2\mathring{\rm A}
is the moiré lattice constant.

A.2
Coulomb interaction and intra-orbital Hund’s coupling

The widely studied Coulomb interaction in MATBG is

H_{I1}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{\mathbf{r}}\sum_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}V(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\left[\rho^{(\eta\eta)}(\mathbf{r})\rho^{(\eta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime})}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})+\rho^{(\eta\overline{\eta})}(\mathbf{r})\rho^{(\overline{\eta}\eta^{\prime})}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\right]\ ,

(12)

where

\displaystyle\rho^{(\eta\eta^{\prime})}(\mathbf{r})=

\displaystyle\Omega_{\rm G}\sum_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}s}\sum_{l}\sum_{\beta,\mathcal{L}_{l\beta}\ni\mathbf{r}}e^{-i(\eta\mathbf{K}_{l}-\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}_{l})\cdot\mathbf{r}}w_{l\beta,\alpha}^{(\eta)*}(\mathbf{r})w_{l\beta,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\eta^{\prime})}(\mathbf{r})f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s}

(13)

and

V(\mathbf{r})=U_{\xi}\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{\sqrt{(\mathbf{r}/\xi)^{2}+n^{2}}}

(14)

is the double-gate-screened Coulomb interaction.
Here
\xi
is the distance between the two gates,
U_{\xi}=e^{2}/(4\pi\epsilon\xi)
, and
\epsilon\approx 6
is the dielectric constant.
For
\xi=10
nm, there is
U_{\xi}=24
meV.
This interaction respects a valley-U(1) symmetry.
Bilinear terms due to the normal order form of operators are omitted because they can be absorbed into the chemical potential for the single-site problem.
The Fourier transformation of
V(\mathbf{r})
is

V(\mathbf{r})=\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}V(\mathbf{q})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{r}},\qquad V(\mathbf{q})=\pi\xi^{2}U_{\xi}\frac{\tanh(\xi|\mathbf{q}|/2)}{\xi|\mathbf{q}|/2}\ .

(15)

The projected intra-valley scattering interaction between
\rho^{(\eta\eta)}
and
\rho^{(\eta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime})}
has been calculated in section S3B of the supplementary material of Ref.
Song and Bernevig 2022
.
It has the form

H_{I1}=\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}=U\frac{\hat{N}(\hat{N}-1)}{2},\qquad U\approx 58\mathrm{meV}\ ,

(16)

where
\hat{N}
is the particle number operator.
There is
no
other term,
e.g.
, Hund’s coupling, contributed by the intra-valley scattering due to the symmetry of MATBG.
One can see discussions around Eq. (S135) of the supplementary material of Ref.
Song and Bernevig 2022
for the proof.

Now we consider the inter-valley scattering interaction between
\rho^{(\eta\overline{\eta})}
and
\rho^{(\overline{\eta}\eta)}
.
Due to the large momentum transfer
\sim 2\mathbf{K}_{l}
, this interaction is strongly suppressed and usually neglected.
Here we discuss it in more details.
The relevant Fourier component for this interaction is
V(\pm 2\mathbf{K}_{l}+\mathbf{q})\approx V(\pm 2\mathbf{K}_{l})=\frac{\pi\xi U_{\xi}}{|\mathbf{K}_{l}|}\approx 4.4\times 10^{3}\mathrm{meV\!\cdot\!\mathring{A}^{2}}
.
As
V(\pm 2\mathbf{K}_{l}+\mathbf{q})
is almost
\mathbf{q}
-independent, it gives a
\delta
-like interaction on the microscopic graphene lattice.
The projected interaction on the moiré orbitals can be estimated as
\frac{4427\mathrm{meV\cdot\mathring{A}^{2}}}{\Omega_{\rm M}}\approx 0.3
meV, with
\Omega_{\rm M}=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}a_{\rm M}^{2}\approx 1.560\times 10^{4}\mathring{\rm A}^{2}
being the moiré unit cell area.
Therefore, this inter-valley scattering interaction contributed by
V(\mathbf{r})
is indeed much weaker compared to the intra-valley one.

Another usually omitted Coulomb interaction is the on-site Hubbard repulsion
U_{0}
of the
p_{z}
orbital of carbon atom.

U_{0}
is estimated as large as 9.3eV
Wehling et al. 2011
, which, however, is still smaller than the critical values for the spin-liquid phase (
3.5t
) and anti-ferromagnetic phase (
(4.5\pm 0.5)t
) of graphene as the hopping
t\approx 2.8
eV is also large.
We find that
U_{0}
leads to a non-negligible Hund’s coupling in MATBG.
The microscopic interaction is

H_{I2}=\frac{U_{0}}{2}\sum_{lss^{\prime}}\sum_{\mathbf{r}}\psi_{l\mathbf{r}s}^{\dagger}\psi_{l\mathbf{r}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\psi_{l\mathbf{r}s^{\prime}}\psi_{l\mathbf{r}s}\ .

(17)

Projecting it onto the
f
-orbitals, we obtain

\displaystyle H_{I2}=

\displaystyle\frac{U_{0}\Omega_{\rm G}^{2}}{2}\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in\mathcal{L}_{l\beta}}\sum_{\alpha_{1,2,3,4}}\sum_{\eta_{1,2,3,4}}\delta_{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2},\eta_{3}+\eta_{4}}w_{l\beta,\alpha_{1}}^{(\eta_{1})*}(\mathbf{r})w_{l\beta,\alpha_{2}}^{(\eta_{2})*}(\mathbf{r})w_{l\beta,\alpha_{3}}^{(\eta_{3})}(\mathbf{r})w_{l\beta,\alpha_{4}}^{(\eta_{4})}(\mathbf{r})f_{\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha_{3}\eta_{3}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha_{4}\eta_{4}s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{U_{0}\Omega_{\rm G}}{2}\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\alpha_{1,2,3,4}}\sum_{\eta_{1,2,3,4}}\delta_{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2},\eta_{3}+\eta_{4}}f_{\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha_{3}\eta_{3}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha_{4}\eta_{4}s}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\ w_{l\beta,\alpha_{1}}^{(\eta_{1})*}(\mathbf{r})w_{l\beta,\alpha_{2}}^{(\eta_{2})*}(\mathbf{r})w_{l\beta,\alpha_{3}}^{(\eta_{3})}(\mathbf{r})w_{l\beta,\alpha_{4}}^{(\eta_{4})}(\mathbf{r})\ .

(18)

Notice that
U_{0}\Omega_{\rm G}\approx 4.7\times 10^{4}\mathrm{meV\!\cdot\!\mathring{A}^{2}}
(
U_{0}
taken as 9eV) is one order larger than
V(\pm\mathbf{K}_{l})
.
As the Wannier functions have the
C_{3z}
eigenvalue
e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\eta(-1)^{\alpha-1}}
, quasi-angular momentum conservation gives the constraint

\eta_{1}(-1)^{\alpha_{1}-1}+\eta_{2}(-1)^{\alpha_{2}-1}=\eta_{3}(-1)^{\alpha_{3}-1}+\eta_{4}(-1)^{\alpha_{4}-1}\mod 3\ .

(19)

Using the Gaussian wave-functions in
eqs.

9
and
10
and the valley-U(1),
C_{3z}
symmetries, we find there are only two types of matrix elements in
H_{I2}
.
We summarize them in the following table

\begin{tabular}[]{c|c|c|c|c|c}&$\alpha_{1}$&$\alpha_{2}$&$\alpha_{3}$&$\alpha_{4}$&$\eta$'s\\
\hline\cr$J_{\rm H}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}=\eta_{3}+\eta_{4}$\\
\hline\cr$J_{\rm H}^{\prime}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\overline{\alpha}_{1}$&$\overline{\alpha}_{1}$&$\eta_{1}=\overline{\eta}_{2}$, $\eta_{3}=\overline{\eta}_{4}$\\
\hline\cr$J_{\rm H}^{\prime}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\overline{\alpha}_{1}$&$\overline{\alpha}_{1}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\eta_{1}=\eta_{4}$, $\eta_{2}=\eta_{3}$\\
\hline\cr$J_{\rm H}^{\prime}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\overline{\alpha}_{1}$&$\alpha_{1}$&$\overline{\alpha}_{1}$&$\eta_{1}=\eta_{3}$, $\eta_{2}=\eta_{4}$\end{tabular}\ .

(20)

The two parameters are

\displaystyle J_{\rm H}=

\displaystyle U_{0}\Omega_{\rm G}\sum_{l\beta}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}|w_{l\beta,\alpha_{1}}^{(\eta_{1})}(\mathbf{r})|^{2}|w_{l\beta,\alpha_{1}}^{(\eta_{2})}(\mathbf{r})|^{2}=2U_{0}\Omega_{\rm G}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\left[\left(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{2}}{2\pi\lambda_{1}^{2}}\right)^{2}e^{-2\frac{\mathbf{r}^{2}}{\lambda_{1}^{2}}}+\left(\frac{\alpha_{2}^{2}}{2\pi\lambda_{2}^{4}}\right)^{2}\mathbf{r}^{4}e^{-2\frac{\mathbf{r}^{2}}{\lambda_{2}^{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U_{0}\Omega_{\rm G}\left[\frac{\alpha_{1}^{4}}{4\pi\lambda_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\alpha_{2}^{4}}{8\pi\lambda_{2}^{2}}\right]\ ,

(21)

\displaystyle J_{\rm H}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle U_{0}\Omega_{\rm G}\sum_{l\beta}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}|w_{l\beta,\alpha_{1}}^{(\eta_{1})}(\mathbf{r})|^{2}|w_{l\beta,\overline{\alpha}_{1}}^{(\eta_{3})}(\mathbf{r})|^{2}=4U_{0}\Omega_{\rm G}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\left[\left(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{2}}{2\pi\lambda_{1}^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{\mathbf{r}^{2}}{\lambda_{1}^{2}}}\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{2}^{2}}{2\pi\lambda_{2}^{4}}\right)\mathbf{r}^{2}e^{-\frac{\mathbf{r}^{2}}{\lambda_{2}^{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U_{0}\Omega_{\rm G}\frac{\alpha_{1}^{2}\alpha_{2}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{\pi(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2})^{2}}\ .

(22)

The projected
H_{I2}
can be written as

\displaystyle H_{I2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha ss^{\prime}}\sum_{\eta_{1,2,3,4}}\delta_{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2},\eta_{3}+\eta_{4}}\bigg[\frac{J_{\rm H}}{2}f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta_{3}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta_{4}s}+\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{3}\overline{\eta}_{4}}\cdot f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\overline{\eta}_{1}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}_{4}s^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta_{4}s}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{3}}\cdot f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s}+\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{4}}\cdot f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta_{2}s}\bigg]\ .

(23)

The parameters in
eq.

11
give
J_{\rm H}=0.3590\times 10^{-3}U_{0}
, and
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{\rm H}=0.1392\times 10^{-3}U_{0}
.
We can also calculate the two parameters using the numerical Wannier functions constructed in Ref.
Song and Bernevig 2022
, which give

J_{\rm H}\approx 0.3284\times 10^{-3}U_{0},\qquad J_{\rm H}^{\prime}\approx 0.1029\times 10^{-3}U_{0}\approx\frac{1}{3}J_{\rm H}\ .

(24)

One can see that the intra-orbital Hund’s coupling is stronger than the inter-orbital Hund’s coupling.

We can rewrite the
J_{\rm H}
term in
section

A.2
as

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha ss^{\prime}}\sum_{\eta_{1,2}}\left[\frac{J_{\rm H}}{2}f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s}+\frac{J_{\rm H}}{2}f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta_{2}s}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{J_{\rm H}}{2}\hat{N}+\frac{J_{\rm H}}{2}\sum_{\alpha}\hat{N}_{\alpha}^{2}+J_{\rm H}\hat{N}-\frac{J_{\rm H}}{2}\sum_{\alpha ss^{\prime}}\sum_{\eta_{1,2}}f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta_{1}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta_{2}s}\ .

(25)

Using the relation
\sum_{\mu=0,x,y,z}\varsigma^{\mu}_{s_{1}s_{2}}\varsigma^{\mu}_{s_{3}s_{4}}=2\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}
, the above expression equals

\displaystyle\frac{J_{\rm H}}{2}\hat{N}+\frac{J_{\rm H}}{4}\sum_{\alpha}\hat{N}_{\alpha}^{2}-J_{\rm H}\sum_{\alpha}\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{\alpha}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{\alpha}\ ,

(26)

where

\hat{N}_{\alpha}=\sum_{\eta s}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta s},\qquad\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{ss^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}\ ,

(27)

and
N=\sum_{\alpha}N_{\alpha}
.
Similarly, we can rewrite the third and forth terms in
section

A.2
as

\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{4}\sum_{\alpha}\hat{N}_{\alpha}\hat{N}_{\overline{\alpha}}-J_{\rm H}^{\prime}\sum_{\alpha}\hat{\bf S}_{\alpha}\cdot\hat{\bf S}_{\overline{\alpha}}\ .

(28)

However, the second term in
section

A.2
cannot be written in terms of charge and spin operators.
In summary,
H_{I2}
can be written as

\displaystyle H_{I2}=\frac{J_{\rm H}}{2}\hat{N}+\sum_{\alpha}\left[\frac{J_{\rm H}}{4}\hat{N}_{\alpha}^{2}+\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{4}\hat{N}_{\alpha}\hat{N}_{\overline{\alpha}}-J_{\rm H}\hat{\bf S}_{\alpha}^{2}-J_{\rm H}^{\prime}\hat{\bf S}_{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{S}_{\overline{\alpha}}\right]+\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\sum_{\alpha ss^{\prime}\eta\eta^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta^{\prime}s}\ .

(29)

A.3
A_{1}
,
B_{1}
-phonon mediated inter-valley anti-Hund’s coupling *

Ref.
Wu et al. 2018
studied the
A_{1}
,
B_{1}
phonon mediated inter-valley attractive interaction

H_{I3}=-g\sum_{l}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}ss^{\prime}}\sum_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\left(1-\eta\eta^{\prime}\right)\psi_{l\beta\eta s}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{r})\psi_{l\beta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{r})\psi_{l\overline{\beta}^{\prime}\overline{\eta}^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})\psi_{l\overline{\beta}\overline{\eta}s}(\mathbf{r})\ .

(30)

\psi_{l\beta\eta s}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{r})
is a continuous version of
eq.

8
that is limited to the valley
\eta
and sub-lattice
\beta

\psi_{l\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{r})=\sum_{\alpha}e^{-i\eta\mathbf{K}_{l}\cdot\mathbf{r}}w_{l\beta,\alpha}^{(\eta)*}(\mathbf{r})f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}\ .

(31)

g=\lambda_{\rm RG}\times 6.9\times 10^{3}\mathrm{meV\!\cdot\!\mathring{A}^{2}}
is the coupling constant and
\lambda_{\rm RG}\approx 3.2
is an enhancement factor due to the renormalization effect from higher energy (
\gtrsim 200
meV) electron and phonon states
Basko and Aleiner 2008
.
This renormalization effect is also considered in Ref.
Kwan et al. 2023
to obtain the T-IVC (time-reversal inter-valley coherent) state at the fillings
\nu=\pm 2
.
The projected
H_{I3}
is calculated in Ref.
Wang et al. 2024
.
Here we directly give the results

H_{I3}=-\frac{J_{\rm d}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\beta\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\beta}\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\beta\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}-\frac{J_{\rm e}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}\ ,

(32)

with
J_{\rm d}=\lambda_{\rm RG}\times 1.3
meV,
J_{\rm e}=\lambda_{\rm RG}\times 1.2
meV.
One can see that
H_{I3}
favors inter-valley spin-singlets.
Thus we call
J_{\rm d,e}
the inter-valley anti-Hund’s couplings.
As
|J_{\rm d}-J_{\rm e}|\ll J_{\rm d}
, in this work we assume
J_{\rm d}=J_{\rm e}=\lambda_{\rm RG}\times 1.3
meV and rewrite the interaction as

\displaystyle H_{I3}=

\displaystyle-\frac{J_{\rm A}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}-\frac{J_{\rm A}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}-\frac{J_{\rm A}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{J_{\rm A}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\beta\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\beta\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\beta\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}-\frac{J_{\rm A}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}\ ,

(33)

J_{\rm A}=\lambda_{\rm RG}\times 1.3
meV, where the subscript A stands for anti-Hund’s coupling.
Using the relation
\sum_{\mu=0,x,y,z}\varsigma^{\mu}_{s_{1}s_{2}}\varsigma^{\mu}_{s_{3}s_{4}}=2\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}
, we can rewrite
H_{I3}
as

H_{I3}=\frac{J_{\rm A}}{4}\sum_{\eta}\hat{N}_{\eta}\hat{N}_{\overline{\eta}}+J_{\rm A}\sum_{\eta}\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{\eta}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{\overline{\eta}}-\frac{J_{\rm A}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}\ ,

(34)

with

\displaystyle\hat{N}_{\eta}=\sum_{\alpha s}f^{\dagger}_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\alpha\eta s},\qquad\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{\eta}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha ss^{\prime}}f^{\dagger}_{\alpha\eta s}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{ss^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}\ ,

(35)

being total charge and spin operators in the valley
\eta
, respectively.

A.4
The
\rm U(1)^{\times 3}\times SU(2)
symmetry and the two-electron states

Adding up
H_{I1,2,3}
, we have

\displaystyle H_{I1}+H_{I2}+H_{I3}=

\displaystyle U\frac{\hat{N}(\hat{N}-1)}{2}+\frac{J_{\rm H}}{2}\hat{N}+\frac{J_{\rm H}}{4}(\hat{N}_{1}^{2}+\hat{N}_{2}^{2})+\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\hat{N}_{1}\hat{N}_{2}+\frac{J_{\rm A}}{2}\hat{N}_{+}\hat{N}_{-}

\displaystyle-J_{\rm H}(\hat{\bf S}_{1}^{2}+\hat{\bf S}_{2}^{2})-J_{\rm H}^{\prime}(\hat{\bf S}^{2}-\hat{\bf S}_{1}^{2}-\hat{\bf S}_{2}^{2})+J_{\rm A}(\hat{\bf S}^{2}-\hat{\bf S}_{+}^{2}-\hat{\bf S}_{-}^{2})

\displaystyle-\frac{J_{\rm A}-J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}+\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}\ .

(36)

We have made use of the relations

2\hat{\bf S}_{1}\cdot\hat{\bf S}_{2}=\hat{\bf S}^{2}-\hat{\bf S}_{1}^{2}-\hat{\bf S}_{2}^{2}
and

2\hat{\bf S}_{+}\cdot\hat{\bf S}_{-}=\hat{\bf S}^{2}-\hat{\bf S}_{+}^{2}-\hat{\bf S}_{-}^{2}
, where
\hat{\bf S}
is the total spin operator, in the derivation.
\hat{\bf S}_{\pm}
here are
\hat{\bf S}_{\eta}
for
\eta=\pm
and one should not confuse them with the raising and lowering operators.

We note that this interaction enjoys a
\rm U(1)^{\times 3}\times SU(2)
continuous symmetry. The three U(1) factors are generated by

\displaystyle\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}\zeta^{0}\ \textrm{(charge)},\qquad\sigma^{0}\tau^{z}\zeta^{0}\ \textrm{(valley)},\qquad\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}\zeta^{0}\ \textrm{(orbital angular momentum)},

(37)

and the SU(2) factor is generated by

\displaystyle\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}\zeta^{x,y,z}\ \textrm{(global spin rotation)}\ .

(38)

In particular, the orbital angular momentum U(1) emerges due to the following reason: although in the lattice model, the orbital angular momentum is only conserved modulo 3 (generated by
C_{3z}=e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}}
), on the effective Anderson impurity, any bilinear terms and quadratic terms that respect
C_{3z}
must also respect a continuous rotation symmetry generated by
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}
.

We now study all the two-electron eigenstates,
F^{\dagger}_{i}|0\rangle
(
i=1,\cdots,28
) of
section

A.4
, where
F_{i}^{\dagger}
is a bilinear of two
f^{\dagger}
operators, and also stands for an eigen-channel for two-particle scatterings.
We normalize
F_{i}^{\dagger}
such that
\langle 0|F_{i}F_{i}^{\dagger}|0\rangle=1
, and label them with
[\rho,j]
, where
\rho
denotes a representation of the
D_{6}
space group, and
j=0,1
denotes the total SU(2) spin.
Note that the valley U(1) charge is also conserved, but as it does not commute with
C_{2z}\in D_{6}
, we have not analyzed how it is represented yet.
As it will turn out, all the levels that are to be obtained belong to one of the two following cases: Either 1)
\rho
is by itself an irreducible representation (irrep) of
D_{6}
, and transforms trivially under valley U(1) (zero valley charge); or 2)
\rho
is a summation of two irreps of
D_{6}
, but they are related to each other by a valley U(1) action.
For both cases,
[\rho,j]
forms an irrep of the entire symmetry group, with both
D_{6}
and the valley U(1) taken into consideration.

To solve the two-particle levels of
section

A.4
, notice that the third row of
section

A.4
only acts on two electrons that are in the same orbital and the opposite valleys.
The states

[E_{2},0],\qquad E=U-J_{\rm A}+2J_{\rm H}^{\prime},\qquad\frac{f_{\alpha+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{\alpha+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\qquad(\alpha=1,2)\ ,

(39)

have
N_{\alpha}=N_{\eta}=1
,
\hat{\bf S}^{2}=0
,
\hat{\bf S}_{\alpha}^{2}=\hat{\bf S}_{\eta}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(1+\frac{1}{2})
, and do not feel the third row of
section

A.4
, which would render
N_{\alpha}
no longer good quantum numbers.
Its energy can be directly calculated as

E=U+J_{\rm H}+\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm H}+\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm H}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm A}-\frac{3}{2}J_{\rm H}+\frac{3}{2}J_{\rm H}^{\prime}-\frac{3}{2}J_{\rm A}
, and can be verified to form the
[E_{2},0]
irrep.
The states

[E_{1},1],\qquad E=U+J_{\rm A},\qquad f_{\alpha+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}-\uparrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\quad f_{\alpha+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}-\downarrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\quad\frac{f_{\alpha+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}-\downarrow}^{\dagger}+f_{\alpha+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\qquad(\alpha=1,2)\ ,

(40)

have
N_{\alpha}=N_{\eta}=1
,
\hat{\bf S}^{2}=1(1+1)
,
\hat{\bf S}_{\alpha}^{2}=\hat{\bf S}_{\eta}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(1+\frac{1}{2})
, and its energy can be calculated as

E=U+J_{\rm H}+\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm H}+\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm H}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm A}-\frac{3}{2}J_{\rm H}-\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm H}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm A}
.
Similarly, we can obtain other eigenstates that are annihilated by the third row of
section

A.4

[E_{1}+E_{2},0],\qquad E=U+2J_{\rm H},\qquad f_{\alpha\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\qquad(\alpha=1,2\;\eta=\pm)\ ,

(41)

[A_{1}+B_{1},0],\qquad E=U+2J_{\rm H}^{\prime}\qquad\frac{f_{1\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{1\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{2\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\qquad(\eta=\pm)\ ,

(42)

[A_{2}+B_{2},1],\qquad E=U,\qquad f_{1\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\quad f_{1\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{2\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\quad\frac{f_{1\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}+f_{1\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{2\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\qquad(\eta=\pm)\ .

(43)

The singlet states
f_{\alpha+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{\alpha+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha-\uparrow}^{\dagger}
would have the energy
E=U+2J_{\rm H}-J_{\rm A}
if the third row of
section

A.4
vanished.
The third row of
section

A.4
scatters the singlet state with
\alpha=1
to the singlet state with
\alpha=2

\displaystyle-\frac{J_{\rm A}-J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}\frac{f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{1+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle=-\left(J_{\rm A}-J_{\rm H}^{\prime}\right)\frac{f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{2+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle

(44)

\displaystyle\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}\frac{f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{1+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle=J_{\rm H}^{\prime}\frac{f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{2+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle

(45)

and vice versa.
The two singlet states then form the bonding and anti-bonding states

[A_{1},0],\qquad E=U+2J_{\rm H}-2J_{\rm A}+2J_{\rm H}^{\prime},\qquad\frac{f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\downarrow}^{\dagger}+f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{1+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\uparrow}^{\dagger}-f_{2+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{2}|0\rangle,

(46)

[B_{2},0],\qquad E=U+2J_{\rm H}-2J_{\rm H}^{\prime},\qquad\frac{f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{1+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\uparrow}^{\dagger}+f_{2+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{2}|0\rangle\ .

(47)

The triplet states
f_{\alpha+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha-\uparrow}^{\dagger}
,
f_{\alpha+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha-\downarrow}^{\dagger}
,

f_{\alpha+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha-\downarrow}^{\dagger}+f_{\alpha+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha-\uparrow}^{\dagger}

would have the energy
E=U+J_{\rm A}
if the third row of
section

A.4
vanished.
The third row of
section

A.4
scatters the triplet states with
\alpha=1
to the triplet states with
\alpha=2
,
e.g.
,

\displaystyle-\frac{J_{\rm A}-J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}\cdot f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\uparrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle=(J_{\rm A}-J_{\rm H}^{\prime})\cdot f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\uparrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle

(48)

\displaystyle\frac{J_{\rm H}^{\prime}}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}{\eta}s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}\cdot f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\uparrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle=J_{\rm H}^{\prime}\cdot f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\uparrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle

(49)

and vice versa.
The two triplet states then form the bonding and anti-bonding states

[A_{2},1],\quad E=U,\quad\frac{f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\uparrow}^{\dagger}-f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\quad\frac{f_{1+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{2+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\downarrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\quad\frac{f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\downarrow}^{\dagger}+(\uparrow\leftrightarrow\downarrow)}{2}|0\rangle,

(50)

[B_{1},1],\quad E=U+2J_{\rm A},\quad\frac{f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\uparrow}^{\dagger}+f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\quad\frac{f_{1+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\downarrow}^{\dagger}+f_{2+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\downarrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\quad\frac{f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\downarrow}^{\dagger}+f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\downarrow}^{\dagger}+(\uparrow\leftrightarrow\downarrow)}{2}|0\rangle\ .

(51)

Assuming
J_{\rm H}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{3}J_{\rm H}
(
eq.

24
), we find that the two-electron ground states must be one of
eqs.

43
,
50
,
39
and
46
.
To be concrete, the ground states are

|\Phi_{d,\alpha}\rangle=\frac{f_{\alpha+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{\alpha+\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}-\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\quad(\alpha=1,2),\quad E=U-J_{\rm A}+\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{J}_{H},\quad\text{if}\;\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm A}<J_{\rm H}<\frac{3}{2}J_{\rm A}\ ,

(52)

|\Phi_{s}\rangle=\frac{f_{1+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{1-\downarrow}^{\dagger}+f_{2+\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{2-\downarrow}^{\dagger}-(\uparrow\leftrightarrow\downarrow)}{2}|0\rangle,\quad E=U-2J_{\rm A}+\frac{8}{3}\mathcal{J}_{H},\quad\text{if}\;J_{\rm H}<\frac{1}{2}J_{\rm A}\ ,

(53)

and the triplets (
eqs.

43
and
50
) if
J_{\rm H}>\frac{3}{2}J_{\rm A}
.
According to the crystalline symmetries in
eq.

5
, the singlets
\Phi_{d,\alpha}
in
eq.

52
transform as
d_{x^{2}-y^{2}}
and
d_{xy}
orbitals under operations in the
D_{6}
point group, belonging to the
E_{2}
representation; the singlet
\Phi_{s}
in
eq.

53
transforms as the
s
orbital and belongs to the
A_{1}
representation.
Using the parameters
U_{0}=9
eV
Wehling et al. 2011
,
\lambda_{\rm RG}=3.2

Basko and Aleiner 2008
, there are
J_{\rm A}=4.16
meV,
J_{\rm H}=2.96
meV, and the ground states are the
d
-wave states.

A.5
Flattened interaction and the

\rm U(1)^{\times 4}\!\times\!SU(2)^{\times 2}
symmetry

section

A.4
is difficult to address analytically when coupled to a bath of itinerant electrons.
To simplify the problem, we study an alternative interaction Hamiltonian
H_{I}
that captures the main features of
section

A.4
.
We require
H_{I}
to satisfy the following conditions:

1.

H_{I}
should reproduce the correct two-electron ground states
|\Phi_{d,\alpha}\rangle
(
eq.

52
), which are
inter-valley inter-orbital spin singlets
that belong to the
E_{2}
representation of the point group
D_{6}
.

2.

The two-electron spectrum of
H_{I}
should be positive definite due to the large Coulomb repulsion
U
. In other words,
H_{I}
itself does
not
have a pairing channel.

3.

The gap between the two-electron ground states and excited states should be at the order of
J_{\rm A}
.

4.

H_{I}
should have as high as possible symmetry for the sake of analytical convenience.

We find the following
H_{I}
can match these requirements

\displaystyle H_{I}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta ss^{\prime}}\big[U_{1}\cdot f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}+U_{2}\cdot f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}+U_{3}\cdot f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\eta s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}

\displaystyle\qquad+U_{4}\cdot f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s}-\mathcal{J}\cdot f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}s}\big]\ ,

(54)

where
\mathcal{J}\sim J_{\rm A}
is an inter-valley inter-orbital anti-Hund’s coupling that favors the
|\Phi_{d,\alpha}\rangle
states,
U_{1}=U-\mathcal{J}
,
U_{2}=U_{3}=U_{4}=U
.
Making use of the relation
\sum_{\mu=0,x,y,z}\varsigma^{\mu}_{s_{1}s_{2}}\varsigma^{\mu}_{s_{3}s_{4}}=2\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}
, we can rewrite
H_{I}
as

H_{I}=-\frac{U_{4}}{2}\hat{N}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha\eta}\left(\left(U_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{J}\right)\hat{N}_{\alpha\eta}\hat{N}_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}}+U_{2}\cdot\hat{N}_{\alpha\eta}\hat{N}_{\alpha\overline{\eta}}+U_{3}\cdot\hat{N}_{\alpha\eta}\hat{N}_{\overline{\alpha}\eta}+U_{4}\cdot\hat{N}_{\alpha\eta}^{2}+2\mathcal{J}\cdot\hat{\bf S}_{\alpha\eta}\cdot\hat{\bf S}_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}}\right)\ ,

(55)

where

\hat{N}_{\alpha\eta}=\sum_{s}f^{\dagger}_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\alpha\eta s},\qquad\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{\alpha\eta}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{ss^{\prime}}f^{\dagger}_{\alpha\eta s}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{ss^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}\ ,

(56)

are the charge and spin in the orbital
\alpha
and valley
\eta
, respectively, and
\hat{N}
is the total charge operator.
In order to calculate the eigenstates of
H_{I}
, we expand
H_{I}
as

\displaystyle H_{I}=

\displaystyle-\frac{U_{4}}{2}\hat{N}+\left(U_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{J}\right)\left(\hat{N}_{1+}\hat{N}_{2-}+\hat{N}_{2+}\hat{N}_{1-}\right)+U_{2}\left(\hat{N}_{1+}\hat{N}_{1-}+\hat{N}_{2+}\hat{N}_{2-}\right)+U_{3}\left(\hat{N}_{1+}\hat{N}_{2+}+\hat{N}_{1-}\hat{N}_{2-}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{U_{4}}{2}(\hat{N}_{1+}^{2}+\hat{N}_{1-}^{2}+\hat{N}_{2+}^{2}+\hat{N}_{2-}^{2})+\mathcal{J}(\hat{\bf S}_{1+}+\hat{\bf S}_{2-})^{2}+\mathcal{J}(\hat{\bf S}_{1-}+\hat{\bf S}_{2+})^{2}-\mathcal{J}(\hat{\bf S}_{1+}^{2}+\hat{\bf S}_{1-}^{2}+\hat{\bf S}_{2+}^{2}+\hat{\bf S}_{2-}^{2})\ .

(57)

The energies can be directly read from this expression once the good quantum numbers are known.

H_{I}
has an
\rm U(1)^{\otimes 4}\!\times\!SU(2)^{\otimes 2}
symmetry.
The four U(1) factors are generated by

\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{0}\;\text{(charge)},\quad\sigma^{0}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{0}\;\text{(valley)},\quad\sigma^{z}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{0}\;\text{(orbital)},\quad\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{0}\;\text{(angular momentum)}\ .

(58)

We call
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{0}
angular momentum because
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}
is the quasi-angular momentum of the
C_{3z}
operator (
eq.

5
), which is now promoted to a continuous rotation symmetry.
The two SU(2) factors are generated by

\frac{\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}+\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}}{2}\varsigma^{x,y,z},\qquad\frac{\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}-\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}}{2}\varsigma^{x,y,z},

(59)

respectively.
They are independent spin rotations in the
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}=1
and
-1
flavors, respectively.

We now calculate all the two-electron eigenstates of
eq.

55
.
First, we classify the two-electron states into angular momentum sectors.
If the total angular momentum is 0, there must be one state in the
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}=1
flavor and one in the
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}=-1
flavor.
Due to the two independent SU(2) rotations, all these states should be spin-degenerate.
We find the energies and eigenstates are

E=U_{2},\qquad f_{\alpha+s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha-s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\qquad(\alpha=1,2,\quad s,s^{\prime}=\uparrow\downarrow)\ ,

(60)

E=U_{3},\qquad f_{1\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{2\eta s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\qquad(\eta=+,-,\quad s,s^{\prime}=\uparrow\downarrow)\ .

(61)

The degeneracy between different orbitals and valleys are protected by the discrete symmetries (
eq.

5
).
If the total angular momentum is 2, the two electrons must occupy
(\alpha,\eta)=(1,+)
or
(2,-)
flavors.
If both particles occupy the same
\alpha,\eta
, they must form a singlet; otherwise they form a singlet and a triplet.
The same analyses also apply to the sector with total angular momentum -2.
We find the energies and eigenstates as

E=U_{4},\qquad f_{\alpha\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\qquad(\alpha=1,2,\quad\eta=+,-)\ ,

(62)

E=U_{1}-\mathcal{J},\qquad\frac{f_{\alpha\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{\alpha\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\qquad((\alpha,\eta)=(1,+),(1,-))\ ,

(63)

E=U_{1}+\mathcal{J},\qquad f_{\alpha\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}\uparrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\quad f_{\alpha\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}\downarrow}^{\dagger}|0\rangle,\quad\frac{f_{\alpha\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}\downarrow}^{\dagger}+f_{\alpha\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\qquad((\alpha,\eta)=(1,+),(1,-))\ .

(64)

The degeneracies between different valleys and orbitals are protected by the discrete symmetries in
eq.

5
.
As there is no accidental degeneracy in the two-electron spectrum,
eq.

55
is already in the most generic form allowed by the
\rm U(1)^{\otimes 4}\!\times\!SU(2)^{\otimes 2}
symmetry.

We choose
U_{1}=U-\mathcal{J}
,
U_{2}=U_{3}=U_{4}=U
,
\mathcal{J}\sim J_{\rm A}
such that the inter-valley inter-orbital singlets have the energy
U-2\mathcal{J}
, while all the other states have the energy
U
.
Such parameterized
H_{I}
can be thought as a “flattened” interaction where all the excited two-electron states are made degenerate.

One can also design a flattened interaction
H_{I}^{\prime}
with the intra-orbital
s
-wave singlet (
eq.

46
) and
B_{2}
singlet (
eq.

47
) as the ground states.
For a single site problem,
H_{I}^{\prime}
is equivalent to
H_{I}
because they are related by the gauge transformation
f_{\alpha-s}\to f_{\overline{\alpha}-s}
,
f_{\alpha+s}\to f_{\alpha+s}
.
Thus, all the formal discussions on the single impurity problem with
H_{I}
also apply to the one with
H_{I}^{\prime}
.
However,
H_{I}
and
H_{I}^{\prime}
lead to physically different pairings because the lattice model is not unchanged under this gauge transformation.

H_{I}^{\prime}
can be used to study the
s
-wave pairing.
(The
B_{2}
pairing should have a higher energy than
s
-wave pairing on the lattice model because it cannot open a full gap.)

A.6
Negligible inter-site interactions

In this subsection, we show that the inter-site interactions are all negligible.
We have considered three different microscopic origins of interactions: 1) long-range gated Coulomb repulsion of 2D electron gas, 2) the atomic Hubbard
U_{0}
, and 3) the
A_{1}
,
B_{1}
-phonons.

For 1),
Song and Bernevig 2022
have shown that inter-site interaction must take the Hubbard form as the on-site
U
, namely, only in a density-density manner, which only counts the
f
-electron number of each site. The strength is estimated as
U^{\rm inter-site}\approx 2.3\mathrm{meV}
.
As the giant on-site
U
has already frozen the
f
-charge of each site to a fixed number,
U^{\rm inter-site}
will act as an identity in all low-energy configurations, hence irrelevant.
This is to be contrasted with the on-site
J_{\rm A,H}
, which does split the low-energy configuration with a fixed
f
-electron number, hence strongly influencing the Kondo physics.
Correspondingly, the quasi-particle interactions should be around the same order as
z^{2}U^{\rm inter-site}
, without a significant renormalization to the corresponding vertex, which is also to be contrasted with the (anti-)Hund’s splittings, which receive receives strong renormalization away from
z^{2}J_{\rm A,H}
, to the universal values determined by
T_{\rm K}
.
With
z=0.2\sim 0.3
,
z^{2}U^{\rm inter-site}\approx 0.1\sim 0.2\mathrm{meV}
, which is weak. Moreover, it acts in inter-site pairing channels only, hence cannot affect the energy of on-site pairings.
In Refs.
Chou and Das Sarma 2023a
;
Zhou et al. 2024
;
Hu et al. 2023a
;
Hu et al. 2023b
;
Datta et al. 2023
;
Rai et al. 2023
;
Lau and Coleman 2023
;
Chou and Das Sarma 2023b
,
U^{\rm inter-site}
is either omitted or decomposed in the Hartree channel such that it only shifts the energy levels of
f
electrons.

For 2), we remark that the atomic Hubbard is extremely short-ranged, hence the inter-site strength should be vanishingly weak, due to the highly localized nature of
f
-orbitals.
We carry out a specific estimation below, referring to
eq.

17
.
In general, each
\psi^{\dagger}_{l\mathbf{r}s}
operator can overlap with
f
-orbitals
f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}
on different AA-sites
\mathbf{R}
, with the overlap given by

\displaystyle\psi^{\dagger}_{l\mathbf{r}s}=\sqrt{\Omega_{\rm G}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\eta\alpha}\sum_{\beta,\mathcal{L}_{l,\beta}\ni\mathbf{r}}e^{-i\eta\mathbf{K}_{l}\cdot(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})}w^{(\eta)*}_{l\beta,\alpha}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}

(65)

where the overlap
w^{(\eta)}_{l\beta,\alpha}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})
dacays in a Gaussian form with respect to
|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R}|
.
eq.

8
has only kept the closest AA-site to
\mathbf{r}
.
Inserting
eq.

65
into
eq.

17
, we obtain

\displaystyle H_{I2}

\displaystyle=\frac{U_{0}\Omega_{G}^{2}}{2}\sum_{ss^{\prime}}\sum_{\alpha_{1,2,3,4}}\sum_{\eta_{1,2,3,4}}\delta_{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2},\eta_{3}+\eta_{4}}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}_{4}\alpha_{4}\eta_{4}s}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}_{3}\alpha_{3}\eta_{3}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}_{1}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s}

(66)

\displaystyle~\left[\sum_{\mathbf{r}}\sum_{l\beta}e^{i\mathbf{K}_{l}\cdot(\eta_{1}\mathbf{R}_{1}+\eta_{2}\mathbf{R}_{2}-\eta_{3}\mathbf{R}_{3}-\eta_{4}\mathbf{R}_{4})}w^{(\eta_{4})*}_{l\beta,\alpha_{4}}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R}_{4})w^{(\eta_{3})*}_{l\beta,\alpha_{3}}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R}_{3})w^{(\eta_{2})}_{l\beta,\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R}_{2})w^{(\eta_{1})}_{l\beta,\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R}_{1})\right]

where we used the following fact that, if and only if
\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}=\eta_{3}+\eta_{4}
, all the fast oscillating factors
e^{i\mathbf{K}_{l}\cdot\mathbf{r}}
can neatly cancel, so that the summation over
\mathbf{r}
can produce a finite value.

It suffices to check those inter-site terms that conserve the charge of each site separately, as otherwise two charge excitations that each costs energy
U
would be involved.
Moreover, as the Wannier functions
w^{(\eta)}_{l\beta,\alpha}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})
decay in a Gaussian form with increasing
|\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R}|
, it suffices to check the strengths of the nearest-neighbor bonds.
Two different cases can arise - A)
\mathbf{R}_{1}=\mathbf{R}_{4}=0
,
\mathbf{R}_{2}=\mathbf{R}_{3}=\mathbf{R}
, with
|\mathbf{R}|=a_{M}
, or B)
\mathbf{R}_{1}=\mathbf{R}_{3}=0
,
\mathbf{R}_{2}=\mathbf{R}_{4}=\mathbf{R}
, with
|\mathbf{R}|=a_{M}
.
The type A) preserves the spin SU(2) of each site, and takes a density-density-like form, while type B) swaps the spin.
The strengths are of the same order of magnitude, hence it suffices to evaluate one,
e.g.
the case A).
The strength is given by

\displaystyle U_{0}\Omega_{G}^{2}\left|\sum_{\mathbf{r}}\sum_{l\beta}e^{i\mathbf{K}_{l}\cdot(\eta_{1}\mathbf{R}_{1}+\eta_{2}\mathbf{R}_{2}-\eta_{3}\mathbf{R}_{3}-\eta_{4}\mathbf{R}_{4})}w^{(\eta_{4})*}_{l\beta,\alpha_{4}}(\mathbf{r})w^{(\eta_{3})*}_{l\beta,\alpha_{3}}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})w^{(\eta_{2})}_{l\beta,\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})w^{(\eta_{1})}_{l\beta,\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r})\right|

(67)

\displaystyle\leq U_{0}\Omega_{G}^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{r}}\sum_{l\beta}\left|w^{(\eta_{4})*}_{l\beta,\alpha_{4}}(\mathbf{r})w^{(\eta_{3})*}_{l\beta,\alpha_{3}}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})w^{(\eta_{2})}_{l\beta,\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})w^{(\eta_{1})}_{l\beta,\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r})\right|

Referring to
eq.

9
to
eq.

10
, each Wannier factor can be (approximately) upperbounded by
|w^{(\eta)}_{l\beta,\alpha}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\lambda_{2}}e^{-\frac{(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})^{2}}{2\lambda_{2}^{2}}}
.
The integration over
\mathbf{r}
can be decomposed to along two directions - perpendicular or parallel to
\mathbf{R}
. The perpendicular integration produces a factor of order
\frac{1}{\lambda_{2}}
, but the parallel integration is strongly suppressed by the localization of the Wannier functions (
\frac{\lambda_{2}}{a_{M}}=0.1910
), as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda_{2}^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}y~e^{-\frac{y^{2}}{\lambda_{2}^{2}}-\frac{(y-a_{M})^{2}}{\lambda_{2}^{2}}}\approx 2\times 10^{-7}\times\frac{1}{\lambda_{2}}

(68)

Altogether, the nearest-neighbor strength is approximately
U_{0}\frac{\Omega_{G}^{2}}{\lambda_{2}^{2}}\times 2\times 10^{-7}<10^{-5}\mathrm{meV}
, which is indeed vanishingly weak.

For 3), the
A_{1},B_{1}
-phonons can propagate for a finite range on the microscopic lattice, but the inter-site strengths will still be much weaker than the on-site strengths
\lambda_{\rm RG}\times 1.3\mathrm{meV}
.
The detailed estimation of the phonon-mediated nearest-neighbor interaction can be found in
Wang et al. 2024
, and we summarize the results here - the density-density-like and spin-exchanging interaction strengths are
\lambda_{\rm RG}\times 0.005\mathrm{meV}
and
\lambda_{\rm RG}\times 0.01\mathrm{meV}
, respectively.
They are even smaller than the typical thermal fluctuation at 1K (
k_{B}\times 1\mathrm{K}\approx 0.09\mathrm{meV}
), hence their effects can be completely negligible as well.

Appendix B
Anderson impurity problem with the flattened interaction

B.1
Effective action *

The effective free Hamiltonian of the single impurity problem is
Zhou et al. 2024

H_{0}=\epsilon_{f}\sum_{\eta\alpha s}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\alpha\eta s}+\int_{-D}^{D}d\epsilon\cdot{\epsilon}\cdot d_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\epsilon)d_{\alpha\eta s}(\epsilon)+\int_{-D}^{D}d\epsilon\cdot h(\epsilon)\cdot d_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\epsilon)f_{\alpha\eta s}+\mathrm{H.c.}\ ,

(69)

where
d_{\alpha\eta s}(\epsilon)
, satisfying
\{d_{\alpha\eta s}(\epsilon),d^{\dagger}_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\epsilon^{\prime})\}=\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{ss^{\prime}}\delta(\epsilon-\epsilon^{\prime})
, is an auxiliary bath reproducing the (retarded) hybridization function

\Delta^{(\rm ret)}(\omega)=\pi\int_{-D}^{D}d\epsilon\cdot\delta(\omega-\epsilon)h^{2}(\epsilon)=\pi h^{2}(\omega).

(70)

Hence, one can choose
h(\epsilon)=\sqrt{\frac{\Delta^{\rm(ret)}(\epsilon)}{\pi}}
.

It is convenient to calculate susceptibilities and vertex functions in the path integral formalism.
By introducing the Grassmann variables
f_{\alpha\eta s}(\tau)
, where
\tau
is the imaginary time, and their Fourier transformations

f_{\alpha\eta s}(\tau)=\sqrt{T}\sum_{\omega}f_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega)e^{-i\omega\tau},\qquad f_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega)=\sqrt{T}\int_{0}^{1/T}d\tau\cdot f_{\alpha\eta s}(\tau)e^{i\omega\tau}\ ,

(71)

with
T
being the temperature and
\omega=(2n+1)\pi T
the fermion Matsubara frequency, the partition function can be written as

\mathcal{Z}=\int D[f^{\dagger}f]e^{-S_{0}-S_{I}}\ .

(72)

Here
S_{0}
is the free part

S_{0}=\sum_{\omega}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\omega)(-i\omega+\epsilon_{f}-i\Delta(\omega))f_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega)\ ,

(73)

The
i\Delta(\omega)
term in
S_{0}
is obtained by integrating out the auxiliary bath

\Delta(\omega)=-i\pi\cdot\int_{-D}^{D}d\epsilon\cdot\frac{h^{2}(\epsilon)}{-i\omega+\epsilon}\ .

(74)

One can see that in the low-frequency regime (
|\omega|\ll D
), there is
\Delta(\omega)\approx\Delta_{0}\cdot\mathrm{sgn}(\omega)
.
As the frequency dependence is not relevant, in the rest of this manuscript we consider a flat hybridization function,
i.e.
,

\Delta(\omega)\equiv\Delta_{0}\cdot\mathrm{sgn}(\omega)\ .

(75)

S_{I}
is the interaction part

\displaystyle S_{I}=

\displaystyle\frac{T}{2}\sum_{\nu}\sum_{\alpha\eta}\bigg[\left(U_{1}+\frac{\mathcal{J}}{2}\right){N}_{\alpha\eta}(\nu){N}_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}}(-\nu)+U_{2}\cdot{N}_{\alpha\eta}(\nu){N}_{\alpha\overline{\eta}}(-\nu)+U_{3}\cdot{N}_{\alpha\eta}(\nu){N}_{\overline{\alpha}\eta}(-\nu)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+U_{4}\cdot{N}_{\alpha\eta}(\nu){N}_{\alpha\eta}(-\nu)+2\mathcal{J}\cdot{\bf S}_{\alpha\eta}(\nu)\cdot{\bf S}_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}}(-\nu)\bigg]\ .

(76)

where
\nu=2\pi Tn
is bosonic Matsubara frequency, and

{N}_{\alpha\eta}(\nu)=T\sum_{\omega}\sum_{s}f^{\dagger}_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega+\nu)f_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega),\qquad{\bf S}_{\eta}(\nu)=\frac{T}{2}\sum_{\omega}\sum_{ss^{\prime}}f^{\dagger}_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega+\nu)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{ss^{\prime}}f_{\alpha\eta s^{\prime}}(\omega)\ ,

(77)

are the charge and spin in the orbital valley flavor
(\alpha,\eta)
, respectively.
It may be worth mentioning that the bilinear term (the first term) in
eq.

55
does not appear in
S_{I}
.
When one writes the partition function as a path integral of Grassmann variables, one should write the Hamiltonian in the normal ordered form with respect to the vacuum.
Thus
S_{I}
should be first written in a normal ordered form where all
f
are on right hand side of
f^{\dagger}
.
Then one rewrites
S_{I}
in terms of
\mathbf{S}_{\eta}
and
N_{\eta}
.
However, interchanging Grassmann variables does not yield the bilinear terms as interchanging second-quantized fermion operators.

For later convenience, we formally rewrite
S_{0}
(
eq.

73
) and
S_{I}
(
sections

B.1
and
B.1
) as

\displaystyle S_{0}=\sum_{1}f^{\dagger}(1)(-i\omega_{1}+\epsilon_{f}-i\Delta(\omega_{1}))f(1)

(78)

and

S_{I}=\frac{T}{4}\sum_{1,2,3,4}\delta_{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\omega_{3}+\omega_{4}}\Gamma^{0}(1,2;3,4)f^{\dagger}(1)f^{\dagger}(2)f(3)f(4)\ ,

(79)

respectively, where
f(1)
,
f(2)
are shorthand for
f_{I_{1}}(\omega_{1})
,
f_{I_{2}}(\omega_{2})
,
etc.
, and
I_{i=1,2,3,4}=(\alpha_{i},\eta_{i},s_{i})
are composite indices.
The Arabic numbers can be regarded as composite indices including frequencies.

\Gamma^{0}(1,2;3,4)\equiv\Gamma^{0}_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2};\omega_{3},\omega_{4})
is the anti-symmetrized bare vertex function, which satisfies

\Gamma^{0}(1,2;3,4)=-\Gamma^{0}(2,1;3,4)=-\Gamma^{0}(1,2;4,3)=\Gamma^{0}(2,1;4,3)\ .

(80)

It should give the same interaction as
section

B.1
(or
section

A.5
).
We can read the (not anti-symmetrized) vertex function from
section

A.5

\displaystyle 4\left[\frac{\Gamma^{0}_{U1}}{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}+\frac{\Gamma^{0}_{U2}}{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}+\frac{\Gamma^{0}_{U3}}{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}+\frac{\Gamma^{0}_{U4}}{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}\right]\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 4\frac{\Gamma^{0}_{\mathcal{J}}}{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\ ,

(81)

where the factor 4 is due to the factor
\frac{1}{4}
in
eq.

79
and
\Gamma^{0}_{U1,2,3,4}=U_{1,2,3,4}
and
\Gamma^{0}_{\mathcal{J}}=\mathcal{J}
.
After anti-symmetrization, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}^{0}(0,0;0,0)=

\displaystyle\left[{\Gamma^{0}_{U1}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}+{\Gamma^{0}_{U2}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}+{\Gamma^{0}_{U3}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}+{\Gamma^{0}_{U4}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times[\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}-\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}]

\displaystyle-\Gamma^{0}_{\mathcal{J}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}[\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}-\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}]\ .

(82)

B.2
Quasi-particle and vertex function *

The Matsubara Green’s function is defined as

\mathcal{G}(\omega)=-\langle f_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega)f^{\dagger}_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega)\rangle=-\langle e^{-S_{I}}f_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega)f^{\dagger}_{\alpha\eta s}(\omega)\rangle_{0C}\ ,

(83)

where
\langle\cdots\rangle
is the average weighted by
e^{-S_{0}-S_{I}}
, and
\langle\cdots\rangle_{0C}
is the average weighted by
e^{-S_{0}}
, and with only the connected diagrams included.
Due to the
\rm U(1)^{\otimes 4}\!\times\!SU(2)^{\otimes 2}
symmetry and the discrete symmetries (
eq.

5
),
\mathcal{G}
must be proportional to an identity in the
\alpha
,
\eta
,
s
indices.
One can formally write
\mathcal{G}
as

\mathcal{G}(\omega)=\frac{1}{i\omega+i\Delta_{0}\cdot\mathrm{sgn}(\omega)-\epsilon_{f}-\Sigma(\omega)}\ .

(84)

The self-energy satisfies the Hermitian condition
\Sigma(\omega)=\Sigma^{*}(-\omega)
.
If the ground state is a Fermi liquid, then
\Sigma(\omega)
behaves as

\Sigma(\omega)=\Sigma(0)+(1-z^{-1})i\omega+\mathcal{O}(\omega^{2})

(85)

around zero-frequency, where
\Sigma(0)
is a real number and

z=(1-\partial_{\omega}\Sigma(\omega))^{-1}|_{\omega=0}

(86)

is the quasi-particle weight.
According to the Friedel sum rule
Shiba 1975
;
Yoshimori 1976
, the total occupation is related to the zero-frequency Green’s function

\frac{\nu_{f}+4}{8}=\frac{1}{\pi}\mathrm{Im}\ln\mathcal{G}(0^{+})=\frac{1}{\pi}\arccos\frac{\epsilon_{f}+\Sigma(0)}{\sqrt{(\epsilon_{f}+\Sigma(0))^{2}+\Delta_{0}^{2}}}\ .

(87)

For later convenience, we define the scattering phase shift

\delta_{f}=\frac{\nu_{f}+4}{8}\pi=\arccos\frac{\epsilon_{f}+\Sigma(0)}{\sqrt{(\epsilon_{f}+\Sigma(0))^{2}+\Delta_{0}^{2}}}.

(88)

It is 0 when the
f
orbital is empty (
\epsilon_{f}+\Sigma(0)\to\infty
),
\frac{\pi}{2}
at half-filling (
\epsilon_{f}+\Sigma(0)=0
), and
\pi
when
f
orbital is fully occupied (
\epsilon_{f}+\Sigma(0)\to-\infty
).
The density of states (per flavor) at the Fermi level, or the spectral height at zero-energy, is

A(0)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\mathrm{Im}[\mathcal{G}(0^{+})]=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{\Delta_{0}}{(\epsilon_{f}+\Sigma(0))^{2}+\Delta_{0}^{2}}=\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\ .

(89)

One can see that
A(0)
is completely determined by
\delta_{f}
and the hybridization
\Delta_{0}
, and is independent of the interaction.

The full anti-symmetrized vertex function
\Gamma(1,2;3,4)
is defined by the following skeleton diagrams up to infinite orders of the bare interaction

\includegraphics[width]{vertex}\ .

(90)

Here the black dots represent the bare interaction (denoted as
\Gamma^{0}
) and the solid lines are the full Green’s functions with self-energy corrections.
For now we are only interested in the zero frequency part of
\Gamma(00;00)
.
Notice that
\Gamma^{0}
, read from
H_{I}
in
section

A.5
, is already in the most generic form allowed by the symmetries because
H_{I}
has no accidental degeneracy.
Thus,
\Gamma(00;00)
must have the same form as
\Gamma^{0}
,
i.e.
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}(0,0;0,0)=

\displaystyle\left[{\Gamma_{U1}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}+{\Gamma_{U2}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}+{\Gamma_{U3}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}+{\Gamma_{U4}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times[\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}-\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{J}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}[\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}-\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{4}}\delta_{\eta_{2}\eta_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}]\ ,

(91)

except that the bare values
\Gamma^{0}_{U1,2,3,4}
,
\Gamma^{0}_{\mathcal{J}}
are replaced by renormalized values
\Gamma_{U1,2,3,4}
,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{J}}
.

It is convenient to define an effective field theory for the low-energy quasi-particle excitations
Hewson 1993b
;
Hewson 2001
.
We define the quasi-particle operator as
\widetilde{f}=z^{-\frac{1}{2}}f
and rewrite the action as
S_{\rm qp}+S_{\rm c}
, with

S_{\rm qp}=\sum_{\omega}\widetilde{f}^{\dagger}_{I}(\omega)(-i\omega-i\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\cdot\mathrm{sgn}(\omega)+\widetilde{\epsilon}_{f})\widetilde{f}_{I}(\omega)+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{1,2,3,4}\delta_{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\omega_{3}+\omega_{4}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(1,2;3,4)\widetilde{f}^{\dagger}(1)\widetilde{f}^{\dagger}(2)\widetilde{f}(3)\widetilde{f}(4)\ ,

(92)

\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}=z\Delta_{0},\qquad\widetilde{\epsilon}_{f}=z\epsilon_{f}+z\Sigma(0),\qquad\widetilde{\Gamma}_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}^{0}(\omega_{1}\omega_{2};\omega_{3}\omega_{4})=z^{2}\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}(00;00)\ ,

(93)

S_{\rm c}=\sum_{\omega}\widetilde{f}^{\dagger}_{I}(\omega)(-i\lambda_{0}\omega+\lambda_{1})\widetilde{f}_{I}(\omega)+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{1234}\Lambda(1,2;3,4)\widetilde{f}^{\dagger}(1)\widetilde{f}^{\dagger}(2)\widetilde{f}(3)\widetilde{f}(4)\ ,

(94)

\lambda_{0}=z-1,\qquad\lambda_{1}=-z\Sigma(0),\qquad\Lambda(1,2;3,4)=z^{2}\Gamma^{0}_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}(00;00)-\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}(00;00)\ .

(95)

The decomposition of
S
into
S_{\rm qp}
and
S_{\rm c}
is exact.

S_{\rm qp}
describes the low-energy Fermi liquid fixed point, and
S_{\rm c}
- the counter term - guarantees that there is no further renormalization to
S_{\rm qp}

Hewson 1993b
;
Hewson 2001
.
One can define the renormalized interaction parameters as

\widetilde{U}_{1,2,3,4}=z^{2}\Gamma_{U1,2,3,4},\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}=z^{2}\Gamma_{\mathcal{J}}\ .

(96)

\lambda_{0,1}
and
\Lambda
can be expressed in powers of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
,
i.e.
,
\lambda_{i}=\sum_{n}\lambda_{i}^{(n)}
and
\Lambda_{i}=\sum_{n}\Lambda_{i}^{(n)}
, where
n
represents the order of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
.
These expressions should be determined order by order in such a way that they cancel all the further renormalizations,
i.e.
, the quasi-particle self-energy
\widetilde{\Sigma}(\omega)
and the quasi-particle full vertex function
\widetilde{\Gamma}
satisfies

\widetilde{\Sigma}(0)=0,\qquad\frac{\partial\widetilde{\Sigma}}{\partial i\omega}\bigg|_{\omega=0}=0,\qquad\widetilde{\Gamma}(00;00)=\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}=z^{2}\Gamma(00;00)\ .

(97)

We will carry out this perturbation calculation in
section

B.7
.
One should be aware that
\widetilde{\Sigma}
can have higher-order frequency-dependence such as
\omega^{2}
terms and
\widetilde{\Gamma}
can also have frequency dependence.

B.3
Susceptibilities

We now consider the susceptibility

\chi^{AB}(\nu)=\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\cdot\langle\hat{A}(\tau)\hat{B}(0)\rangle e^{i\nu\tau}

(98)

where
\nu
is the bosonic Matsubara frequency,

\hat{A}=\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}A_{I_{1},I_{2}}f_{I_{2}}
,

\hat{B}=\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}B_{I_{1},I_{2}}f_{I_{2}}
.
We will only focus on the static response,
i.e.
,
\chi^{AB}(\nu\to 0^{+})
.
It describes the response
\delta\langle\hat{A}\rangle
with respect to a static external field
-h^{B}\hat{B}
,
i.e.
,
\chi^{AB}(0^{+})=\frac{\partial\langle\hat{A}\rangle}{\partial h^{B}}
.

\sigma^{0,z}\tau^{0,z}

\sigma^{\pm}\tau^{0,z}

\sigma^{0,z}\tau^{\pm}

\sigma^{\pm}\tau^{\pm}

\sigma^{\pm}\tau^{\mp}

\tau^{z}
charge

0

0

\pm 2

\pm 2

\mp 2

\sigma^{z}
charge

0

\pm 2

0

\pm 2

\pm 2

\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}
charge

0

\pm 2

\pm 2

0

0

Independent

\sigma^{0,z}\tau^{0,z}

\sigma^{x}\tau^{0,z}

\sigma^{0,z}\tau^{x}

\sigma^{x}\tau^{x}

Table 1:
Nine independent charge neutral spin-0 channels in the susceptibility of the four-orbital Anderson impurity model with the flattened interaction.

The symmetries can help us identify independent channels of the susceptibilities, since
\chi^{AB}
should be block-diagonal in the irreducible representations.
We first classify the spin-0 operators
\sigma^{0,x,y,z}\tau^{0,x,y,z}\varsigma^{0}
.
According to the U(1) symmetry generators in
eq.

58
, these operators can be labeled by (i) valley (
\tau^{z}
) charge, (ii) orbital (
\sigma^{z}
) charge, and (iii) angular momentum (
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}
).
We summarize the U(1) charges of the sixteen operators in
table

1
.
Operators in different columns do not couple to each other due to the U(1) charges conservation.
For each column, the susceptibility is in principle a matrix.
We can make use of the discrete symmetries in
eq.

5
to further diagonalize the susceptibility for each column:

1.

The four operators
\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}
,
\sigma^{0}\tau^{z}
,
\sigma^{z}\tau^{0}
,
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}
in the first column form four different one-dimensional representations of the
D_{2}
point group generated by
C_{2x}=\sigma^{x}
,
C_{2z}=\sigma^{x}\tau^{x}
.
Therefore, each of them is an eigenmode of the susceptibility.
(We do not need to consider the
C_{3z}
symmetry here because it is already promoted to the continuous rotation generated by the angular momentum
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}
.)

2.

The four operators in the second column can be recombined to the hermitian matrix basis
\sigma^{x,y}\tau^{0,z}
. The four hermitian operators do not couple to each other because they belong to four different one-dimensional representations of the
D_{2}
point group.
The susceptibilities for
\sigma^{y}\tau^{0,z}
must be same as as that of
\sigma^{x}\tau^{0,z}
due to the orbital U(1) symmetry generated by
\sigma^{z}
.
Therefore, we only need to consider the two representative channels
\sigma^{x}\tau^{0,z}
.

3.

Analyses for the second column also apply to the third column, except that the roles of valley and orbital are exchanged.

4.

The four operators in the fourth and fifth columns can be recombined to the hermitian matrix basis
\sigma^{x,y}\tau^{x,y}
. Again, they belong to four different one-dimensional representations of the
D_{2}
point group and do not couple to each other. Due to the valley-U(1) (
\tau^{z}
) and orbital-U(1) (
\sigma^{z}
), we only need to consider the susceptibility of
\sigma^{x}\tau^{x}
.

We second classify the spin-1 operators.
Due to the global SU(2) symmetry, we only need to consider the
\sigma^{\mu}\tau^{\nu}\varsigma^{z}
(
\mu,\nu=0,x,y,z
) operators.
As the valley-U(1), orbital-U(1), angular momentum, and
D_{2}
point group operators are independent of spin, all the discussions in the last paragraph apply to
\sigma^{\mu}\tau^{\nu}\varsigma^{z}
.
Therefore, there are at most nine independent spin-1 channels, which are given by the operators in the last row of
table

1
multiplied by
\varsigma^{z}
.
We now show that the spin-1 operators only contribute three new independent channels:

1.

Spin-1 operators descended from the first column of
table

1
are
\sigma^{0,z}\tau^{0,z}\varsigma^{z}
.
Nevertheless, the
\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{z}
and
\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{z}
operators are related to each other by the successive global and relative SU(2) rotations
e^{i\frac{\pi}{4}\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{y}}\cdot e^{-i\frac{\pi}{4}\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{y}}
(
eq.

59
), thus they must have the same susceptibility.
Similarly, the other two operators
\sigma^{z}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{z}
and
\sigma^{0}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{z}
are also related by
e^{i\frac{\pi}{4}\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{y}}\cdot e^{-i\frac{\pi}{4}\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{y}}
.
Therefore, we can choose
\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{z}
and
\sigma^{0}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{z}
as the representative operators.

2.

Spin-1 operators descended from the second column of
table

1
are
\sigma^{x}\tau^{0,z}\varsigma^{z}
.
The successive orbital U(1) and relative SU(2) rotations
e^{i\frac{\pi}{4}\sigma^{z}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{0}}\cdot e^{-i\frac{\pi}{4}\sigma^{z}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{z}}
transform them to spin-0 operators
\sigma^{x}\tau^{z,0}\varsigma^{0}
. Thus these operators do not contribute new channels.

3.

Spin-1 operators descended from the third column of
table

1
do not contribute new channels for the same reason as above.

4.

The only spin-1 operator descended from the last two columns of
table

1
is
\sigma^{x}\tau^{x}\varsigma^{z}
.

In summary, there are twelve independent channels in the susceptibility.
We use the notation
O^{\mu\nu\rho}=\sigma^{\mu}\tau^{\nu}\varsigma^{\rho}
to represent these operators.
The twelve eigenmodes can be chosen as

\displaystyle O^{000},\quad O^{0z0},\quad O^{z00},\quad O^{zz0},\quad O^{00z},\quad

(99)

\displaystyle O^{0zz},\quad O^{x00},\quad O^{xz0},\quad O^{0x0},\quad O^{zx0},\quad O^{xx0},\quad O^{xxz}\ .

Operators in the first row are generators of the continuous symmetry group, whereas operators in the second row are not.

We now apply perturbation calculation of
\chi
to the first order of
S_{I}
.
Applying Fourier transformation to
eq.

98
, we obtain

\chi^{O}(\nu)=T\sum_{1234}\delta_{\omega_{1}-\omega_{2},-\nu}\delta_{\omega_{3}-\omega_{4},\nu}\left\langle e^{-S_{I}}f^{\dagger}(1)O(1,2)f(2)f^{\dagger}(3)O(3,4)f(4)\right\rangle_{0C}\ ,

(100)

where
i=1,2,3,4
are composite indices and
O(i,j^{\prime})=O_{I_{i},I_{j}}
.
To the zeroth order of
S_{I}
, the susceptibility is

\displaystyle\chi^{O(0)}(\nu)=

{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(4)\vbox to16.00002pt{}{}=-T\sum_{12}\delta_{\omega_{1}-\omega_{2},-\nu}O(1,2)O(2,1)\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{1})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T\cdot\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]\sum_{\omega}\frac{1}{\omega+\Delta(\omega)+i\epsilon_{f}}\cdot\frac{1}{\omega+\nu+\Delta(\omega+\nu)+i\epsilon_{f}}\ ,

(101)

where
\mathrm{tr}[\cdots]
represents the trace over
\alpha,\eta,s
.
To the zeroth order of
\nu
we have

\displaystyle\chi^{O(0)}(0^{+})=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\frac{1}{(\omega+\Delta(\omega)+i\epsilon_{f})^{2}}=\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\left(\frac{1}{(\omega+\Delta_{0}+i\epsilon_{f})^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\omega+\Delta_{0}-i\epsilon_{f})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\pi\cdot(\Delta_{0}^{2}+\epsilon_{f}^{2})}=\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]\cdot\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\ ,

(102)

where
\delta_{f}=\pi(\nu_{f}+4)/8
is the scattering phase shift.
To the first order of
S_{I}
, we have

\displaystyle\chi^{O(1)}(0^{+})=

\displaystyle-T^{2}\sum_{1234}\sum_{5678}\delta_{\omega_{1}-\omega_{2},-\nu}\delta_{\omega_{3}-\omega_{4},\nu}O(1,2)O(3,4)\times\frac{1}{4}\delta_{\omega_{5}+\omega_{6},\omega_{7}+\omega_{8}}\Gamma^{0}(5,6;7,8)

\displaystyle\times\left\langle f^{\dagger}(1)f(2)f^{\dagger}(3)f(4)f^{\dagger}(5)f^{\dagger}(6)f(7)f(8)\right\rangle_{0C}\ .

(103)

Applying Wick’s theorem, there is

\displaystyle\chi^{O(1)}(\nu)=

\displaystyle-T^{2}\sum_{1234}\sum_{5678}\delta_{\omega_{1}-\omega_{2},-\nu}\delta_{\omega_{3}-\omega_{4},\nu}O(1,2)O(3,4)\times\frac{1}{4}\delta_{\omega_{5}+\omega_{6},\omega_{7}+\omega_{8}}\Gamma^{0}(5,6;7,8)

\displaystyle\times 4\times\hbox to5.97pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.98613pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
