\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(8)\vbox to22.00002pt{}{}\ .

(104)

The factor 4 comes from equivalent contractions, which are equal to one another because
\Gamma^{0}
is fully anti-symmetrized.
The contraction equals

(-1)^{2}\delta_{52}\delta_{64}\delta_{73}\delta_{81}\cdot\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{1})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{2})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{3})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{4})\ .

(105)

Hence,

\displaystyle\chi^{O(1)}(\nu)=

\displaystyle-T^{2}\sum_{1234}\delta_{\omega_{1}-\omega_{2},-\nu}\delta_{\omega_{3}-\omega_{4},\nu}O(1,2)O(3,4)\cdot\Gamma^{0}(2,4;3,1)\cdot\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{1})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{2})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{3})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{4})

(106)

Since
O
and
\Gamma^{0}
does not depend on the frequency, we can separate the summations over frequency and matrix indices.
Introducing the factor

\kappa[O,\Gamma^{0}]=\frac{1}{\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]}\sum_{I_{1,2,3,4}}O_{I_{1},I_{2}}O_{I_{3},I_{4}}\Gamma^{0}_{I_{2},I_{4};I_{3},I_{1}}(00;00)=\frac{1}{\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]}\sum_{I_{1,2,3,4}}O_{I_{4},I_{1}}O_{I_{3},I_{2}}\Gamma^{0}_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}(00;00)\ ,

(107)

we have

\chi^{O(1)}(0^{+})=-\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]\cdot\kappa[O,\Gamma^{0}]\int\frac{d\omega_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{d\omega_{3}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{(\omega_{1}+\Delta(\omega_{1})+i\epsilon_{f})^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{(\omega_{3}+\Delta(\omega_{3})+i\epsilon_{f})^{2}}=-\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]\kappa[O,\Gamma^{0}]\left(\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\right)^{2}\ .

(108)

Adding up the zeroth and first-order contributions, we obtain

\chi^{O}\approx\chi^{O(0)}+\chi^{O(1)}=\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\left(1-\kappa[O,\Gamma^{0}]\cdot\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\right)\ .

(109)

eq.

109
is not very useful as
\Gamma^{0}
is usually large and cannot be treated as a perturbation.
However, we can obtain the quasi-particle susceptibility by replacing
\Delta_{0}
and
\Gamma^{0}
with the renormalized values
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}=z\Delta_{0}
and
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}=z^{2}\Gamma(00;00)

\widetilde{\chi}^{O}\approx\mathrm{tr}[O^{2}]\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\kappa[O,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]\cdot\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\right)\ .

(110)

Considering
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
in
eq.

92
is already renormalized, which means it is the full quasi-particle vertex function at zero frequency
\widetilde{\Gamma}(00;00)
, there will be no higher order correction to the above equation.
But
eq.

110
is also not obviously exact because it omits the frequency dependence of the
\widetilde{\Gamma}
.
It also omits the frequency dependence of the quasi-particle self-energy
\widetilde{\Sigma}(\omega)
(beyond quasi-particle weight correction),
e.g.
,
\omega^{2}
terms in
\widetilde{\Sigma}(\omega)
.
Nevertheless, we will prove in the next subsection through Ward identities that
eq.

110
equals the
exact susceptibilities
of the
bare
particles if
O
is a generator of the continuous symmetry group.

We now determine the factor
\kappa[O,\Gamma^{0}]
(
eq.

107
) for the twelve channels in
eq.

99
.
In the first six channels there are
I_{1}=I_{4}
and
I_{2}=I_{3}
in the expression
eq.

107
.
The involved vertex function elements are

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{I_{1}I_{2};I_{2}I_{1}}(00;00)=\widetilde{U}_{1}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}+\widetilde{U}_{2}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}+\widetilde{U}_{3}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}+\widetilde{U}_{4}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}(1-\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}})+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}

(111)

It is direct to obtain

\kappa[O^{000},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=2\widetilde{U}_{1}+2\widetilde{U}_{2}+2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\ ,

(112)

\kappa[O^{0z0},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=-2\widetilde{U}_{1}-2\widetilde{U}_{2}+2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\ ,

(113)

\kappa[O^{z00},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=-2\widetilde{U}_{1}+2\widetilde{U}_{2}-2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\ ,

(114)

\kappa[O^{zz0},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=2\widetilde{U}_{1}-2\widetilde{U}_{2}-2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\ ,

(115)

\kappa[O^{00z},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=-\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\ ,

(116)

\kappa[O^{0zz},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=-\widetilde{U}_{4}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\ .

(117)

For the seventh and eighth operators
O^{x00}
,
O^{xz0}
, there are

\alpha_{4}=\overline{\alpha}_{1}
,
\eta_{4}=\eta_{1}
,
s_{4}=s_{1}
,

\alpha_{3}=\overline{\alpha}_{2}
,
\eta_{3}=\eta_{2}
,
s_{3}=s_{2}

in the expression
eq.

107
. The involved vertex function elements are

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}(00;00)=-\widetilde{U}_{3}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{1}}\ .

(118)

It is direct to obtain

\kappa[O^{x00},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=\kappa[O^{xz0},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=-\widetilde{U}_{3}\ .

(119)

For the ninth and tenth operators
O^{0x0}
,
O^{zx0}
, the roles of orbital and valley are exchanged, thus the
\kappa
factors should be the same except the inter-orbital repulsion
\widetilde{U_{3}}
is replaced by the inter-valley repulsion
\widetilde{U}_{2}
:

\kappa[O^{0x0},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=\kappa[O^{zx0},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=-\widetilde{U}_{2}\ .

(120)

For the eleventh and twelfth operators
O^{xx0}
,
O^{xxz}
, there are

\alpha_{4}=\overline{\alpha}_{1}
,
\eta_{4}=\overline{\eta}_{1}
,
s_{4}=s_{1}
,

\alpha_{3}=\overline{\alpha}_{2}
,
\eta_{3}=\overline{\eta}_{2}
,
s_{3}=s_{2}

in the expression
eq.

107
. The involved vertex function elements are

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}(00;00)=-\widetilde{U}_{1}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\cdot\delta_{\alpha_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\overline{\eta}_{2}}\ .

(121)

It is direct to obtain

\kappa[O^{xx0},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=-\widetilde{U}_{1}-2\widetilde{\mathcal{J}},\qquad\kappa[O^{xxz},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=-\widetilde{U}_{1}\ .

(122)

For later reference, we explicitly write down the quasi-particle susceptibilities here.
Taking
O=O^{000}
,
O^{0z0}
,
O^{z00}
,
O^{zz0}
,
O^{00z}
, we obtain the charge, valley, orbital, angular momentum, and spin susceptibilities

\widetilde{\chi}^{c}\approx\chi^{c}=8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(2\widetilde{U}_{1}+2\widetilde{U}_{2}+2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})\right),\qquad\text{(exact)}\ ,

(123)

\widetilde{\chi}^{v}\approx\chi^{v}=8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-2\widetilde{U}_{1}-2\widetilde{U}_{2}+2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})\right),\qquad\text{(exact)}\ ,

(124)

\widetilde{\chi}^{o}\approx\chi^{o}=8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-2\widetilde{U}_{1}+2\widetilde{U}_{2}-2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})\right),\qquad\text{(exact)}\ ,

(125)

\widetilde{\chi}^{a}\approx\chi^{a}=8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(2\widetilde{U}_{1}-2\widetilde{U}_{2}-2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})\right),\qquad\text{(exact)}\ ,

(126)

\widetilde{\chi}^{s}\approx\chi^{s}=8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})\right),\qquad\text{(exact)}\ .

(127)

As the above five channels are given by generators of the continuous symmetry, they are exact susceptibilities of the bare particles, as will be proven in the next subsection.
The remaining channels
O^{0zz}
,
O^{x00}
,
O^{xz0}
,
O^{0x0}
,
O^{zx0}
,
O^{xx0}
,
O^{xxz}
give

\widetilde{\chi}^{0zz}\approx 8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-\widetilde{U}_{4}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})\right)\ ,

(128)

\widetilde{\chi}^{x00}=\widetilde{\chi}^{xz0}\approx 8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-\widetilde{U}_{3})\right)\ ,

(129)

\widetilde{\chi}^{0x0}=\widetilde{\chi}^{zx0}\approx 8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-\widetilde{U}_{2})\right)\ ,

(130)

\widetilde{\chi}^{xx0}\approx 8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-\widetilde{U}_{1}-2\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})\right)\ ,

(131)

\widetilde{\chi}^{xxz}\approx 8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-\widetilde{U}_{1})\right)\ .

(132)

B.4
Ward identities and exact susceptibilities *

We consider a perturbation

\Delta S=\sum_{1}f^{\dagger}(1)V(1)f(1)

(133)

where
V(1)=V_{\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}
is a diagonal matrix and is independent of frequency.
Then the first order correction to the Green’s function can be written as

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{G}(1)=

\displaystyle-\langle f(1)f^{\dagger}(1)(-\Delta S)e^{-S_{I}}\rangle_{0C}

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.3 L 0 26.98 L 0 26.98 L 0 10.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\dagger}(2)\hbox to5.97pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.98613pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.3 L 0 22.83 L 0 22.83 L 0 10.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(2)\vbox to22.00002pt{}{}\cdot\Gamma^{0}(3,4;5,6)\cdot V(2)+\cdots

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\mathcal{G}^{0}(1)]^{2}\cdot V(1)+T\sum_{2}[\mathcal{G}^{0}(1)]^{2}\cdot\Gamma^{0}(1,2;2,1)\cdot[\mathcal{G}^{0}(2)]^{2}\cdot V(2)+\cdots

(134)

The subscript
0C
in the first row represents the connected diagrams, and the factor
4
in the second term of the second row comes from four equivalent contractions.
Summing over all the terms to infinite order, we obtain the
exact
result

\delta\mathcal{G}(1)=[\mathcal{G}(1)]^{2}\cdot V(1)+T\sum_{2}[\mathcal{G}(1)]^{2}\cdot\Gamma(1,2;2,1)\cdot[\mathcal{G}(2)]^{2}\cdot V(2)\ ,

(135)

which can be represented by the skeleton diagram

(136)

Here bare Green’s functions and vertex function are replaced by the full Green’s functions and vertex function respectively.
Writing
\delta\mathcal{G}(1)=-[\mathcal{G}(1)]^{2}\cdot(-V(1)-\delta\Sigma(1))
, we obtain the perturbed self-energy

\delta\Sigma_{I_{1}}(\omega_{1})=\sum_{I_{2}}\int\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}\cdot\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{2},I_{1}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2};\omega_{2},\omega_{1})\cdot[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\cdot V_{I_{2}}\ .

(137)

Here
I_{i}=(\alpha_{i},\eta_{i},s_{i})
is a composite index.
We consider the perturbation term
V_{I}=-h^{O}\cdot O_{I}
with
O
being a diagonal matrix, then there is

\sum_{I_{1}}\frac{\partial\Sigma_{I_{1}}(\omega_{1})}{\partial h^{O}}O_{I_{1}}=-\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}\int\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}\cdot O_{I_{1}}O_{I_{2}}\cdot\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{2},I_{1}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2};\omega_{2},\omega_{1})\cdot[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\ .

(138)

We then consider a gauge transformation
f_{I}^{\prime}(\omega)=f_{I}(\omega+\nu\cdot O_{I})
.
On the one hand, there must be

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{I_{1}}^{\prime}(\omega_{1})-\mathcal{G}(\omega_{1})=\mathcal{G}(\omega_{1}+\nu\cdot O_{I_{1}})-\mathcal{G}(\omega_{1})=-[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{1})]^{2}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega_{1}}\left([\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{1})]^{-1}-\Sigma(\omega_{1})\right)\cdot\nu\cdot O_{I_{1}}+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{1})]^{2}\cdot\frac{\partial[\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{1})]^{-1}}{\partial\omega_{1}}\cdot\nu\cdot O_{I_{1}}+[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{1})]^{2}\frac{\partial\Sigma(\omega)}{\partial i\omega}\cdot i\nu\cdot O_{I_{1}}+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2})

(139)

Here
\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{1})=(i\omega_{1}+i\Delta_{0}\cdot\mathrm{sgn}(\omega_{1})-\epsilon_{f})^{-1}
is the free propagator.
On the other hand, one can rewrite the action
S[f^{\dagger},f]
in terms of
S[f^{\prime\dagger},f^{\prime}]+\Delta S[f^{\prime\dagger},f^{\prime}]
, with the perturbation

\Delta S=\sum_{1}f^{\prime\dagger}(1)(-i\nu\cdot O(1)-i\nu\cdot 2\Delta_{0}\delta(\omega_{1})O(1))f^{\prime}(1)=\sum_{1}f^{\prime\dagger}(1)\left(-\nu\cdot O(1)\partial_{\omega_{1}}[\mathcal{G}^{0}(1)]^{-1}\right)f^{\prime}(1)\ .

(140)

There is no other perturbation term in
\Delta S

as long as
O
is a generator of the continuous symmetry
such that it leaves all the instantaneous terms (
\omega
-independent terms) invariant.
Substituting
V(1)=-\nu\cdot O(1)\partial_{\omega_{1}}[\mathcal{G}^{0}(1)]^{-1}
into
eq.

135
and comparing it to
section

B.4
, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Sigma(\omega_{1})}{\partial i\omega_{1}}\cdot i\nu\cdot O_{I_{1}}=

\displaystyle-\sum_{I_{2}}\int\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{2},I_{1}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2};\omega_{2},\omega_{1})\cdot[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\cdot\nu\cdot O_{I_{2}}\cdot\partial_{\omega_{2}}[\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{2})]^{-1}

(141)

We do not explicitly write
\partial_{\omega_{2}}[\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{2})]^{-1}
as
i+2i\Delta_{0}\delta(\omega_{2})
because it will lead to the ill-defined term
[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\delta(\omega_{2})
, where
\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})
is discontinuous at
\omega_{2}=0
.
This problem comes from abuse of the
\delta
-function.
Here we avoid this problem by rewriting
[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\partial_{\omega_{2}}[\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{2})]^{-1}
as

\displaystyle[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\partial_{\omega_{2}}[\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{2})]^{-1}=[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\left(\partial_{\omega_{2}}[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{-1}+\partial_{\omega_{2}}\Sigma(\omega_{2})\right)=-\partial_{\omega_{2}}\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})+[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\partial_{\omega_{2}}\Sigma(\omega_{2})

(142)

The derivative
\partial_{\omega_{2}}\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})
consists of a continuous part at
\omega_{2}\neq 0
and a
\delta
-function peak at
\omega_{2}=0
that is responsible for the discontinuity of
\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})
,
i.e.,

\partial_{\omega_{2}}\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})=-[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}(i-\partial_{\omega_{2}}\Sigma(\omega_{2}))+(\mathcal{G}(0^{+})-\mathcal{G}(0^{-}))\delta(\omega_{2})\ .

(143)

This expression is correct in the sense that integrating it gives the correct antiderivative.
Then there is

[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\partial_{\omega_{2}}[\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{2})]^{-1}=-(\mathcal{G}(0^{+})-\mathcal{G}(0^{-}))\delta(\omega_{2})+i[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\ .

(144)

Substituting this expression into
eq.

141
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Sigma(\omega_{1})}{\partial i\omega_{1}}\cdot O_{I_{1}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{G}(0^{+})-\mathcal{G}(0^{-})}{2\pi i}\sum_{I_{2}}\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{2},I_{1}}(\omega_{1},0;0,\omega_{1})\cdot O_{I_{2}}-\sum_{I_{2}}\int\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{2},I_{1}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2};\omega_{2},\omega_{1})\cdot[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\cdot O_{I_{2}}

(145)

According to the Friedel sum rule (
eq.

89
), there is

\frac{\mathcal{G}(0^{+})-\mathcal{G}(0^{-})}{2\pi i}=-A(0)=-\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{\pi\Delta_{0}}

(146)

and hence

\frac{\partial\Sigma(\omega_{1})}{\partial i\omega_{1}}\cdot O_{I_{1}}=-\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\sum_{I_{2}}\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{2},I_{1}}(\omega_{1},0;0,\omega_{1})\cdot O_{I_{2}}-\sum_{I_{2}}\int\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{2},I_{1}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2};\omega_{2},\omega_{1})\cdot[\mathcal{G}(\omega_{2})]^{2}\cdot O_{I_{2}}\ .

(147)

Comparing
eq.

147
to
eq.

138
, we have

\frac{\partial\Sigma(\omega_{1})}{\partial i\omega_{1}}\sum_{I_{1}}O_{I_{1}}^{2}=\sum_{I_{1}}\frac{\partial\Sigma_{I_{1}}(\omega_{1})}{\partial h^{O}}O_{I_{1}}-\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}\Gamma_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{2},I_{1}}(\omega_{1},0;0,\omega_{1})\cdot O_{I_{1}}O_{I_{2}}\ .

(148)

Now we consider the response
\overline{O}=\sum_{I}O_{I}\langle f^{\dagger}_{I}f_{I}\rangle

\displaystyle\overline{O}=-\sum_{I}O_{I}\langle f_{I}(-0^{+})f_{I}^{\dagger}(0)\rangle=T\sum_{I}O_{I}\sum_{\omega}e^{i\omega 0^{+}}\mathcal{G}_{I}(\omega)

(149)

to the external field
-h^{O}O_{I}
.

\displaystyle\chi^{O}=\frac{\partial\overline{O}}{\partial h^{O}}=-T\sum_{I}O_{I}\sum_{\omega}e^{i\omega 0^{+}}[\mathcal{G}(\omega)]^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial h^{O}}[h^{O}O_{I}-\Sigma_{I}(\omega)]=T\sum_{I}O_{I}\sum_{\omega}e^{i\omega 0^{+}}[\mathcal{G}(\omega)]^{2}[-O_{I}+\frac{\partial\Sigma_{I}(\omega)}{\partial h^{O}}]\ .

(150)

Making use of the Ward identity
eq.

148
, we can replace the
h
-derivative by the
\omega
-derivative

\displaystyle\chi^{O}=

\displaystyle T\sum_{I}O_{I}^{2}\sum_{\omega}e^{i\omega 0^{+}}[\mathcal{G}(\omega)]^{2}\left[-1+\frac{\partial\Sigma(\omega)}{\partial i\omega}\right]+T\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\sum_{II_{2}}\sum_{\omega}e^{i\omega 0^{+}}[\mathcal{G}(\omega)]^{2}\cdot\Gamma_{I,I_{2};I_{2},I}(\omega,0;0,\omega)O_{I}O_{I_{2}}

(151)

[\mathcal{G}_{I}(\omega)]^{2}\left[-1+\frac{\partial\Sigma(\omega)}{\partial i\omega}\right]
is the continuous part of the Green’s function derivative (
eq.

143
), and it can be rewritten as

[\mathcal{G}_{I}(\omega)]^{2}\left[-1+\frac{\partial\Sigma(\omega)}{\partial i\omega}\right]=\frac{\partial\mathcal{G}(\omega)}{\partial i\omega}-\frac{\mathcal{G}(0^{+})-\mathcal{G}(0^{-})}{i}\delta(\omega)=\frac{\partial\mathcal{G}(\omega)}{\partial i\omega}+2\pi\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\delta(\omega)\ .

(152)

Thus,

\chi^{O}=T\sum_{I}O_{I}^{2}\sum_{\omega}e^{i\omega 0^{+}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{G}(\omega)}{\partial i\omega}+\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\sum_{I}O_{I}^{2}+T\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\sum_{II_{2}}\sum_{\omega}e^{i\omega 0^{+}}[\mathcal{G}(\omega)]^{2}\cdot\Gamma_{I,I_{2};I_{2},I}(\omega,0;0,\omega)O_{I}O_{I_{2}}\ ,

(153)

where the first term vanishes.
Making use of the identity
eq.

138
, we rewrite the above equation as

\displaystyle\chi^{O}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\sum_{I}O_{I}^{2}-\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\cdot\sum_{I}\frac{\partial\Sigma_{I}(0)}{\partial h^{O}}O_{I}=\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\sum_{I}\left(O_{I}^{2}-\frac{\partial\Sigma_{I}(0)}{\partial h^{O}}O_{I}\right)\ .

(154)

Now we have expressed the exact susceptibility in terms of the derivatives of zero-frequency self-energy.
To relate the susceptibility to the vertex function, we replace the
\partial_{h^{O}}\Sigma_{I}(0)
by the
\omega
-derivative via
eq.

148

\displaystyle\chi^{O}=\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\sum_{I}O_{I}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\Sigma(\omega)}{\partial i\omega}\right)_{\omega=0}-\left(\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\right)^{2}\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}\Gamma_{I_{1}I_{2};I_{2}I_{1}}(00;00)\cdot O_{I_{1}}O_{I_{2}}\ .

(155)

Notice that
\left(1-\frac{\Sigma(\omega)}{\partial i\omega}\right)_{\omega=0}
equals inverse of the quasi-particle weight
z^{-1}
, there is

\chi^{O}=\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{z\cdot\pi\Delta_{0}}\left(\sum_{I}O_{I}^{2}-\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta_{f})}{z\cdot\pi\Delta_{0}}\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}z^{2}\Gamma_{I_{1}I_{2};I_{2}I_{1}}(00;00)\cdot O_{I_{1}}O_{I_{2}}\right)\ .

(156)

We now have expressed the
exact
susceptibility in terms of quasi-particle weight
z
, hybridization
\Delta_{0}
, filling
\delta_{f}=\pi(\nu_{f}+4)/8
, and
full vertex function at zero-frequency
.
It is worth emphasizing that this identity applies only when
O
is a generator of the continuous symmetry.
Applying it to the
O
operators discussed in the last subsection, we find the five susceptibilities in Eqs. (
123
) to (
127
) are exact.

B.5
Pairing susceptibilities and irreducible vertex in Cooper channel

Following the same procedure in
section

B.3
, we can calculate the pairing susceptibilities
\chi_{\rm p}
.

One can diagonalize the pairing susceptibilities by enumerating distinct two-electron representations, which have been discussed in detail in
section

A.5
.
There are five non-degenerate two-electron levels (
eqs.

60
,
61
,
62
,
63
and
64
).
To proceed, we choose the following five operators to represent the five channels

\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}O^{0xy}_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{2}}^{\dagger}\qquad\text{(intra-orbital inter-valley singlet)}\ ,

(157)

\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}O^{x0y}_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{2}}^{\dagger}\qquad\text{(inter-orbital intra-valley singlet)}\ ,

(158)

\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}O^{00y}_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{2}}^{\dagger}\qquad\text{(intra-orbital intra-valley singlet)}\ ,

(159)

\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}O^{xxy}_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{2}}^{\dagger}\qquad\text{(inter-orbital inter-valley singlet)}\ ,

(160)

\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}O^{xy0}_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{2}}^{\dagger}\qquad\text{(inter-orbital inter-valley triplet)}\ ,

(161)

where
O^{\mu\nu\rho}=\sigma^{\mu}\tau^{\nu}\varsigma^{\rho}
.
Each of them is chosen as a linear combination of a few degenerate two-electron states in
eqs.

60
,
61
,
62
,
63
and
64
.

To apply
eq.

98
to pairing susceptibility, we should define
\hat{A}=\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{2}}O^{*}_{I_{1},I_{2}}f_{I_{1}}
,

\hat{B}=\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}f_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}O_{I_{1},I_{2}}f^{\dagger}_{I_{2}}
, for
O
matrices given in the above equations.
Notice that
O
is always anti-symmetric.
Fouriering Eq. (
98
),

\chi^{O}_{\rm p}(\nu)=T\sum_{1234}\delta_{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\nu}\delta_{\omega_{3}+\omega_{4},\nu}\left\langle e^{-S_{I}}f(2)O^{*}(1,2)f(1)f^{\dagger}(3)O(3,4)f^{\dagger}(4)\right\rangle_{0C}\ .

(162)

At the zeroth order of
S_{I}
,

\displaystyle\chi^{O(0)}_{\rm p}(\nu)

\displaystyle=T\sum_{1234}\delta_{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\nu}\delta_{\omega_{3}+\omega_{4},\nu}\left(\hbox to5.97pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.98613pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(2)O^{*}(1,2)\hbox to5.97pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.98613pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\dagger}(3)O(3,4)\hbox to5.97pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\dagger}(4)\vbox to16.00002pt{}{}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{12}\left[O^{*}(1,2)O(1,2)-O^{*}(1,2)O(2,1)\right]T\sum_{\omega_{1}}\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{1})\mathcal{G}^{0}(-\omega_{1}+\nu)

\displaystyle\overset{\nu\to 0^{+}}{=}\sum_{12}2\cdot O^{*}(1,2)O(1,2)\cdot\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)

(163)

where we used

\displaystyle T\sum_{\omega}\frac{1}{i\omega-\epsilon_{f}+i\Delta(\omega)}\frac{1}{-i\omega-\epsilon_{f}+i\Delta(-\omega)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2T\sum_{\omega>0}\frac{1}{(\omega+\Delta_{0})^{2}+(\epsilon_{f})^{2}}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\omega\frac{1}{(\omega+\Delta_{0})^{2}+(\epsilon_{f})^{2}}

(164)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi\epsilon_{f}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\arctan\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon_{f}}\right)=\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)

and
O(1,2)=-O(2,1)
.
Thus the zeroth order susceptibility can be written as

\chi^{O(0)}_{\rm p}=2\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[O^{\dagger}O\right]\cdot\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\ .

(165)

At the first order of
S_{I}
,

\displaystyle\chi^{O(1)}_{\rm p}(\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T^{2}\sum_{1234}\delta_{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\nu}\delta_{\omega_{3}+\omega_{4},\nu}O^{*}(1,2)O(3,4)\sum_{5678}\frac{1}{4}\delta_{\omega_{5}+\omega_{6},\omega_{7}+\omega_{8}}\Gamma^{0}(5,6;7,8)

(166)

\displaystyle\times\left\langle f(2)f(1)f^{\dagger}(3)f^{\dagger}(4)f^{\dagger}(5)f^{\dagger}(6)f(7)f(8)\right\rangle_{0C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T^{2}\sum_{1234}\delta_{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\nu}\delta_{\omega_{3}+\omega_{4},\nu}O^{*}(1,2)O(3,4)\sum_{5678}\frac{1}{4}\delta_{\omega_{5}+\omega_{6},\omega_{7}+\omega_{8}}\Gamma^{0}(5,6;7,8)

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(8)\vbox to22.00002pt{}{}

where the
\times 4
factor is contributed by equivalent contractions. The contraction gives

\displaystyle(-1)^{2}\delta_{25}\delta_{16}\delta_{37}\delta_{48}\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{1})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{2})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{3})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{4})\ ,

(167)

thereby,

\displaystyle\chi^{O(1)}_{\rm p}(0^{+})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T^{2}\sum_{1234}O^{*}(1,2)O(3,4)\Gamma^{0}(2,1;3,4)\sum_{\omega_{1},\omega_{3}}\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{1})\mathcal{G}^{0}(-\omega_{1})\mathcal{G}^{0}(\omega_{3})\mathcal{G}^{0}(-\omega_{3})

(168)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[\sum_{1234}O^{*}(1,2)\Gamma^{0}(2,1;3,4)O(3,4)\right]\left(\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\Delta_{0}}(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f})\right)^{2}\ .

Replacing
{\Delta}_{0}
,
\Gamma^{0}
by the effective parameters of quasi-particles
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}
,
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
, we obtain the quasi-particle contributed pairing susceptibilities

\widetilde{\chi}^{O}_{\rm p}\approx 2\cdot\mathrm{tr}[O^{\dagger}O]\cdot\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1-\kappa_{\rm p}[O,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]\cdot\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\right]

(169)

where

\kappa_{\rm p}[O,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]=\frac{1}{2\cdot\mathrm{tr}[O^{\dagger}O]}\sum_{I_{1,2,3,4}}O^{*}_{I_{2},I_{1}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{I_{1},I_{2};I_{3},I_{4}}(0,0;0,0)O_{I_{3},I_{4}}\ .

(170)

One should be aware that
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
equals the exact full vertex function
\widetilde{\Gamma}
of quasi-particles at zero frequency, but the frequency dependence of
\widetilde{\Gamma}
has been omitted in the above equation.
We have also omitted the frequency dependence of the quasi-particle self-energy
\widetilde{\Sigma}(\omega)
(beyond quasi-particle weight correction),
e.g.
,
\omega^{2}
terms in
\widetilde{\Sigma}(\omega)
.
Therefore,
eq.

169
is an
approximate
result.

We find
\kappa_{\rm p}[O,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]
is nothing but the energy (in terms of bare
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
) of the two-electron states defined by
O
.
Readers may directly verify this.
Here we prove this statement.

O
defines a two-electron state
|\Phi\rangle=\sum_{IJ}O_{IJ}f_{I}^{\dagger}f_{J}^{\dagger}|0\rangle
.
Its norm is given by
\langle\Phi|\Phi\rangle=\sum_{IJ}O_{IJ}^{*}O_{IJ}-O_{IJ}^{*}O_{JI}=2\cdot\mathrm{tr}[O^{\dagger}O]
.
Then
\kappa_{\rm p}[O,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}]
is by definition
\langle\Phi|H_{\rm eff}|\Phi\rangle/\langle\Phi|\Phi\rangle
with
H_{\rm eff}
being the effective Hamiltonian defined by
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
.
For the two-electron states given in
eqs.

60
,
61
,
62
,
63
and
64
, where
O
is chosen as

O^{0xy}
,
O^{x0y}
,
O^{00y}
,
O^{xxy}
,
O^{xy0}
, respectively, the corresponding pairing susceptibilities are

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{0xy}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\widetilde{U}_{2}\right]\ ,

(171)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{x0y}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\widetilde{U}_{3}\right]\ ,

(172)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{00y}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\widetilde{U}_{4}\right]\ ,

(173)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{xxy}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left(\widetilde{U}_{1}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right]\ ,

(174)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{xy0}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left(\widetilde{U}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right]\ .

(175)

We consider to decompose
\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}
into a summation of ladder diagrams

(176)

where
\Gamma^{\rm p}
is the two-particle irreducible vertex in the pairing channel.

\Gamma^{\rm p}
cannot be divided into two by cutting two left-going Green’s function lines.
Due to the
\rm U(1)\!\times\!U(1)\!\times\!U(2)\!\times\!U(2)
symmetry,
\Gamma^{\rm p}
must have the same form as
\Gamma
, and can be parameterized by
\Gamma^{\rm p}_{U1,2,3,4}
,
{\Gamma}^{\rm p}_{\mathcal{J}}
.
We define the effective interaction in Cooper channel as
U_{1,2,3,4}^{\rm p}=z^{2}\Gamma^{\rm p}_{U1,2,3,4}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\rm p}=z^{2}\Gamma^{\rm p}_{\mathcal{J}}
.
Neglecting the frequency dependencies of
\Gamma^{\rm p}
and the renormalized self-energy, the pairing susceptibilities can be written in terms of effective interactions as

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{0xy}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1+\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)U_{2}^{\rm p}\right]^{-1}\ ,

(177)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{x0y}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1+\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)U_{3}^{\rm p}\right]^{-1}\ ,

(178)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{00y}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1+\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)U_{4}^{\rm p}\right]^{-1}\ ,

(179)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{xxy}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1+\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left(U_{1}^{\rm p}-\mathcal{J}^{\rm p}\right)\right]^{-1}\ ,

(180)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{xy0}\approx 16\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left[1+\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\left(U_{1}^{\rm p}+\mathcal{J}^{\rm p}\right)\right]^{-1}\ .

(181)

Equaling these equations to Eqs. (
171
) to (
175
), we obtain the effective interactions (in the pairing channel) in terms of the renormalized interactions

U^{\rm p}_{2}=\frac{\widetilde{U}_{2}}{1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\widetilde{U}_{2}}\ ,\qquad U^{\rm p}_{3}=\frac{\widetilde{U}_{3}}{1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\widetilde{U}_{3}}\ ,\qquad U^{\rm p}_{4}=\frac{\widetilde{U}_{4}}{1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)\widetilde{U}_{4}}\ ,

(182)

U^{\rm p}_{1}-{\mathcal{J}}^{\rm p}=\frac{\widetilde{U}_{1}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}}{1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)(\widetilde{U}_{1}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})}\ ,\qquad U^{\rm p}_{1}+{\mathcal{J}}^{\rm p}=\frac{\widetilde{U}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}}{1-\frac{\tan\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f}\right)(\widetilde{U}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})}\ .

(183)

B.6
Asymptotic behavior of exact vertex function

B.6.1
Bethe ansatz as a benchmark

As reviewed in this subsubsection, Ward identity has been used to extract local 1PI previously
Hewson 1993b
, which reproduces the Bethe ansatz results on the one-orbital half-filled Anderson impurity as shown by Ref.
Pandis 2015
.
The charge and spin susceptibilities of this model
Kawakami and Okiji 1982
;
Wiegmann and Tsvelick 1983
are

\displaystyle\chi^{c}=\frac{1}{4\pi U}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}y\cdot e^{-\frac{\pi Uy^{2}}{2\Delta_{0}}}\frac{(U/2\Delta_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(y+\frac{1}{2})^{2}(U/2\Delta_{0})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}}\ .

(184)

\displaystyle\chi^{s}=\frac{1}{4\pi U}\left[2\pi\sqrt{\frac{U}{2\Delta_{0}}}e^{\frac{\pi(U^{2}-4\Delta_{0}^{2})}{8U\Delta_{0}}}+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}ye^{-\frac{\pi Uy^{2}}{2\Delta_{0}}}\frac{(U/2\Delta_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(iy+\frac{1}{2})^{2}(U/2\Delta_{0})^{2}+1/4}\right]\,

(185)

Following
eq.

110
and discussions thereafter, they should be

\displaystyle\chi^{c}=2\frac{1}{\pi\tilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{\tilde{U}}{\pi\tilde{\Delta}_{0}}\right),\chi^{s}=2\frac{1}{\pi\tilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1+\frac{\tilde{U}}{\pi\tilde{\Delta}_{0}}\right)

(186)

when expressed in terms of renormalized interactions. We then have
\tilde{\Delta}_{0}=\frac{4}{\pi(\chi^{c}+\chi^{s})},\tilde{U}=\frac{\pi^{2}\tilde{\Delta}_{0}^{2}(\chi^{s}-\chi^{c})}{4}
. In large
U
limit,
\chi^{c}\ll\chi^{s}
, for which we easily see that
\chi^{c}\ll\tilde{\Delta}_{0}^{-1}=\pi(\chi^{c}+\chi^{s})/4
. Combined with
eq.

186
, this implies
\tilde{U}=\pi\tilde{\Delta}_{0}
. We can also explicitly verify

\displaystyle\lim_{U\to\infty}\frac{\tilde{U}}{\pi\tilde{\Delta}_{0}}=\lim_{U\to\infty}\frac{\pi\tilde{\Delta}_{0}(\chi^{s}-\chi^{c})}{4}=\lim_{U\to\infty}\frac{\chi^{s}-\chi^{c}}{\chi^{s}+\chi^{c}}=1\,.

(187)

B.6.2
The
U\gg\mathcal{J}\gg T_{\rm K}
limit

The
\nu_{f}=\pm 2
states.

We focus on the doped correlated insulators at the total fillings
\nu=\pm(2+\delta\nu)
, where
0\!<\!\delta\nu\!<\!1
.
According to the (approximate) particle-hole symmetry of the problem, we only consider the
\nu=-2
case.
According to the calculations in Ref.
Zhou et al. 2024
;
Hu et al. 2023b
;
Datta et al. 2023
;
Rai et al. 2023
, the strong repulsion interaction will fix
\nu_{f}
around
-2
for
-3<\nu<-2
.
The two-electron states are already discussed in
section

A.5
.
In the
T_{\rm K}\sim\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\ll\mathcal{J}
limit, only the ground states (
eq.

63
) of
H_{I}

\frac{f_{\alpha\eta\uparrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}\downarrow}^{\dagger}-f_{\alpha\eta\downarrow}^{\dagger}f_{\overline{\alpha}\overline{\eta}\uparrow}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\qquad(\alpha\eta=1+,1-),\qquad E=U-2\mathcal{J}\ .

(188)

will participate in the Kondo screening
Nozières and Blandin 1980
.
(See section 6.4 of Ref.
Nozières and Blandin 1980
for the discussion about splittings in atomic levels.)
They transform as
d_{x^{2}-y^{2}}
and
d_{xy}
orbitals under the symmetry operations in
D_{6}
and form the
E_{2}
representation.
We have shown that Eqs. (
123
) to (
127
) are the
exact
susceptibilities of
bare
particles.
The charge (
\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{0}
), the valley-charge (
\sigma^{0}\tau^{z}\varsigma^{0}
), the orbital-charge (
\sigma^{z}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{0}
), and the total spin (
\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{z}
) take fixed values 2, 0, 0, 0 in the ground state manifold, respectively.
Therefore, these degrees of freedom are frozen at the Kondo energy scale
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}
, and the corresponding susceptibilities are not contributed by quasi-particles.
For example, in the one-orbital Anderson impurity model at half-filling, the charge susceptibility
\chi^{c}
given by the Bethe ansatz is much smaller than the quasi-particle density of states at Fermi level (
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}^{-1}
) as mentioned in the previous subsubsection.
In fact,
\chi^{c}\cdot\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\ll 1
is a universal behavior of Fermi liquid in the Kondo regime.
For
eq.

123
to reproduce this correct behavior of
\chi^{c}
, the renormalized interactions in the bracket must cancel the
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}^{-1}
divergence, implying the constraint

2\widetilde{U}_{1}+2\widetilde{U}_{2}+2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}=\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}\ .

(189)

The same argument also applies to valley, orbital, and spin degrees of freedom because they are also frozen at the Kondo energy scale and are not contributed by quasi-particles.
The constraints
\chi^{c,v,o,s}\cdot\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\ll 1
imply

\widetilde{U}_{1}=-\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}},\qquad\widetilde{U}_{2}=\widetilde{U}_{3}=\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}},\qquad\widetilde{U}_{4}=-\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\ .

(190)

The two-electron energies of a generic
H_{I}
are given in
section

A.5
.
Replacing the bare interaction parameters with the renormalized interaction parameters, we can obtain eigenvalues of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
as

\widetilde{U}_{2}=\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\;\text{(intra-orbital inter-valley)},\hskip 18.49988pt\widetilde{U}_{3}=\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\;\text{(inter-valley intra-orbital)}\ ,

(191)

\widetilde{U}_{4}=-\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\;\text{(intra-orbital intra-valley singlet)}\ ,

(192)

\widetilde{U}_{1}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}=-\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\;\text{(inter-valley inter-orbital singlet)},\hskip 18.49988pt\widetilde{U}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}=-\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\;\text{(inter-valley inter-orbital triplet)}\ .

(193)

Here
\delta_{f}=\frac{\pi}{4}
.
One of the last two must be negative.
Therefore, we prove the statement that the renormalized interaction has at least one negative channel.

Unlike the charge, valley, orbital, and spin, the angular momentum (
\sigma^{z}\tau^{0}\varsigma^{z}
) is not quenched in the ground state of
H_{I}
.
Thus, the quasi-particle contributed part of
\chi^{a}
should diverge at the order
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}^{-1}
in the
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\to 0
limit.

\chi^{a}
expressed in terms of
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
is

\chi^{a}=8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(8-4\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\ .

(194)

Requiring
\chi^{a}
non-negative leads to the condition
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\leq 2\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}
.

We can also extract useful conditions from the other quasi-particle susceptibilities in Eqs. (
128
) to (
132
) and (
171
) to (
175
).
Even though they are not related to the exact susceptibilities of the bare particles, they should also be positive for the Fermi liquid theory to be valid.
Then we have the inequalities

\widetilde{\chi}^{0zz}\approx 8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(0+\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}2\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\gtrsim 0,\qquad\widetilde{\chi}^{x00}=\widetilde{\chi}^{xz0}=\widetilde{\chi}^{0x0}=\widetilde{\chi}^{zx0}\approx 8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\cdot\left(2-\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\gtrsim 0\ ,

(195)

\widetilde{\chi}^{xx0}\approx 8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(0+\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}2\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\gtrsim 0,\qquad\widetilde{\chi}^{xxz}=8\frac{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\cdot 0\gtrsim 0\ ,

(196)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{0xy}=\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{x0y}\approx\frac{4}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right)\gtrsim 0,\qquad\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{00y}\approx\frac{4}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(-2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right)\gtrsim 0,

(197)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{xxy}\approx\frac{4}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(-2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right)\gtrsim 0,\qquad\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{xy0}\approx\frac{4}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(-2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right)\gtrsim 0.

(198)

As the susceptibilities are approximate, we use “
\gtrsim
” rather than “
\geq
” in these inequalities.
This requirement leads to

4.6\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\approx 8\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-1\right)\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\lesssim\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\lesssim 4\left(1+\frac{\pi}{2}\right)\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\approx 10.3\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\ .

(199)

We have plotted the estimated range for all the five pairing channels (
\widetilde{U}_{1}\mp\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
,
\widetilde{U}_{2,3,4}
) correspondingt to
eq.

199
in
fig.

6
.
One can directly verify that the inter-orbital inter-valley singlet pairing fluctuation (
\chi^{xxy}_{\rm p}
) is the strongest among all the pairings.
The
universality hypothesis
states that
T_{\rm K}
, or
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}
, is the only energy scale in the Kondo regime, implying that the ratio
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}/\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}
is a universal constant.
Our analyses above show that this ratio is in the range from 4.6 to 10.3.

For later convenience, here we also express the effective interactions in the pairing channel as functions of
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
(
eqs.

182
and
183
)

U^{\rm p}_{2}=U^{\rm p}_{3}\approx\frac{2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}}{1-\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})},\qquad U^{\rm p}_{4}\approx\frac{-2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}}{1-\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})}\ ,

(200)

U^{\rm p}_{1}-\mathcal{J}^{\rm p}\approx\frac{-2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}}{1-\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})},\qquad U^{\rm p}_{1}+\mathcal{J}^{\rm p}\approx\frac{-2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}}{1-\frac{1}{4\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}(-2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})}\ .

(201)

The
\nu_{f}=0
states.

The flattened interaction (
eq.

55
) is designed for the
\nu_{f}=\pm 2
states in the actual MATBG in such a way that it reproduces the correct two-electron ground states, as explained at the beginning of
section

A.5
.
Thus, it may not apply to the
\nu_{f}=0
states in the actual MATBG.
However, here we still discuss the physics at
\nu_{f}=0
for the flattened interaction (
eq.

55
) for theoretical interests.
The four-particle ground state is unique, which can be understood as filling the doubly degenerate
\nu_{f}=-2
ground states simultaneously.
It has

N_{\alpha\eta}=1
,
\mathbf{S}_{\alpha\eta}^{2}=\frac{3}{4}
for all
\alpha\eta
flavors, and
(\mathbf{S}_{1+}+\mathbf{S}_{2-})^{2}=(\mathbf{S}_{1-}+\mathbf{S}_{2+})^{2}=0
.
Its energy can be read from
section

A.5
as
E=6U-2\mathcal{J}
.
Apart from
\chi^{c,s,v,o}\cdot\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\ll 1
, there is also
\chi^{a}\cdot\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\ll 1
in the
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\ll\mathcal{J}
limit, which thus fixes
\widetilde{J}=2\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}=2\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}
, where
\delta_{f}=\frac{\pi}{2}
at
\nu_{f}=0
.
Substituting the renormalized interactions and
\delta_{f}\to\frac{\pi}{2}
into
eqs.

171
,
172
,
173
,
174
and
175
, we have

\tan\delta_{f}\cdot(\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta_{f})=1
,

\widetilde{U}_{2}=\widetilde{U}_{3}=0
,

\widetilde{U}_{4}=\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}
,

\widetilde{U}_{1}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}=-3\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}
,

\widetilde{U}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}=\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}

and hence

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{0xy}=\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{x0y}\approx\frac{16}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1+0\right),\qquad\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{00y}\approx\frac{16}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-1\right),

(202)

\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{xxy}\approx\frac{16}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1+3\right),\qquad\widetilde{\chi}_{\rm p}^{xy0}\approx\frac{16}{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\left(1-1\right)\ .

(203)

One can see that the inter-orbital inter-valley singlet fluctuation is the only one that is promoted by interaction.
However, as
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}
is negligible at
\nu_{f}=0

Zhou et al. 2024
;
Hu et al. 2023a
, the energy scale of renormalized attractive interactions is also negligible.

B.6.3
The
U\gg T_{\rm K}\gg\mathcal{J}
limit

We only focus the states at
\nu_{f}=-2
in this subsubsection.
On the one hand, only the charge degree of freedom is frozen in the
U\gg T_{\rm K}\gg\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\sim T
limit, implying (
eq.

189
)

2\widetilde{U}_{1}+2\widetilde{U}_{2}+2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}=\frac{\pi\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{\sin^{2}\delta_{f}}\ .

(204)

\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
is barely renormalized in the Fermi liquid phase because it is much smaller than the Kondo energy scale.
Thus, there must be
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\approx\mathcal{J}
.
On the other hand, the model has an U(8) symmetry in the
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\to 0
limit.
This approximate U(8) symmetry implies
\widetilde{U}_{1}\approx\widetilde{U}_{2}\approx\widetilde{U}_{3}\approx\widetilde{U}_{4}
.
Therefore, we have

\widetilde{U}_{1}\approx\widetilde{U}_{2}\approx\widetilde{U}_{3}\approx\widetilde{U}_{4}\approx\frac{2\pi}{7}\widetilde{\Delta}_{0},\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}=\mathcal{J}\ .

(205)

The ground state of the renormalized interaction is still the
d
-wave
E_{2}
states (
eq.

63
).
It has the energy

\widetilde{U}_{1}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}=\frac{2\pi}{7}\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\mathcal{J}\ .

(206)

This result implies that the pairing potential should start becoming negative when
\mathcal{J}\sim T_{\rm K}
.
Combined with the discussions in the last subsubsection, this shows that the pairing potential is negative when
T_{\rm K}\ll\mathcal{J}
and remains negative to the regime
T_{\rm K}>\mathcal{J}
as
T_{\rm K}
increases, as shown in
fig.

3
(e).

B.7
The counter term *

In this section we demonstrate how the counter term (
eq.

94
) is determined perturbatively in terms of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
.

The first order
\widetilde{\Gamma}
(
fig.

5
(a)) is

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{(1)}(00;00)=\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(00;00)+\Lambda^{(1)}\ ,

(207)

where
\Lambda^{(1)}
is the first-order counter term.

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{(1)}(00;00)
equals
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(00;00)
by definition (
eq.

97
).
Thus,
\Lambda^{(1)}=0
.

The first order self-energy is given by the diagrams in
fig.

5
(b), where the first term is contributed by
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}
and the second term is contributed by the first-order counter terms
\lambda_{0,1}^{(1)}
.
To derive this self-energy, we consider its correction to the Green’s function

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(1)}(1)=

\displaystyle-\left\langle f(1)\left(-\sum_{2}f^{\dagger}(2)(-\lambda_{0}^{(1)}\omega_{2}+\lambda_{1}^{(1)})f(2)-\frac{T}{4}\sum_{2345}\delta_{\omega_{2}+\omega_{3},\omega_{4}+\omega_{5}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(23;45)f^{\dagger}(2)f^{\dagger}(3)f(4)f(5)\right)f^{\dagger}(1)\right\rangle_{0C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{0}(1)]^{2}\times\left(-\lambda_{0}^{(1)}\omega_{1}+\lambda_{1}^{(1)}+4\frac{T}{4}\sum_{2}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(12;21)\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{0}(2)\right)

(208)

where the factor 4 in the second term of the second row comes from 4 equivalent contractions,
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{0}
is the free propagator of the quasi-particles, and the subscript
0C
means connected diagrams.
The self-energy can be read as

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(1)}(1)=-i\lambda_{0}^{(1)}\omega_{1}+\lambda_{1}^{(1)}+T\sum_{2}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(12;21)\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{0}(2)=-i\lambda_{0}^{(1)}\omega_{1}+\lambda_{1}^{(1)}+\sum_{I_{2}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{I_{1}I_{2};I_{2}I_{1}}\int\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{0}(\omega_{2})e^{i0^{+}\omega_{2}}\ ,

(209)

where the factor
e^{i0^{+}\omega_{2}}
factor is due to the time-ordering in the Green’s function
-\langle f(-0^{+})f^{\dagger}(0)\rangle
such that the creation operator is before the annihilation operator.
The interaction factor is (
eq.

112
)

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{2}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{I_{1}I_{2};I_{2}I_{1}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{I_{1}I_{2}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{I_{1}I_{2};I_{2}I_{1}}=

\displaystyle 2\widetilde{U}_{1}+2\widetilde{U}_{2}+2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\ .

(210)

The frequency integral is

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}e^{i0^{+}\omega_{2}}\frac{1}{i\omega_{2}+i\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}\cdot\mathrm{sgn}(\omega_{2})-\widetilde{\epsilon}_{f}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}e^{i0^{+}\omega_{2}}\frac{1}{i\omega_{2}+i\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\widetilde{\epsilon}_{f}}+c.c.

(211)

Define
z=i\omega_{2}
, the function
\frac{1}{z+i\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\widetilde{\epsilon}_{f}}
is analytical in the half plane
\mathrm{Im}[z]>0
.
We can continuously deform the integral line from
(0,i\infty)
to
(0,-\infty)
, then we have

\int_{0}^{-\infty}\frac{dz}{2\pi i}e^{0^{+}z}\frac{1}{z+i\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}-\widetilde{\epsilon}_{f}}+c.c.=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}dz\cdot e^{0^{+}z}\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}{(z-\widetilde{\epsilon}_{f})^{2}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}^{2}}=\pi\delta_{f}\ .

(212)

Therefore, there is

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(1)}(\omega)=-i\lambda_{0}^{(1)}\omega+\lambda_{1}^{(1)}+\left(2\widetilde{U}_{1}+2\widetilde{U}_{2}+2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\pi\delta_{f}

(213)

The condition
eq.

97
implies

\lambda_{0}^{(1)}=0,\qquad\lambda_{1}^{(1)}=-\left(2\widetilde{U}_{1}+2\widetilde{U}_{2}+2\widetilde{U}_{3}+\widetilde{U}_{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\pi\delta_{f}\ .

(214)

This also implies
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(1)}(\omega)=0
.

Figure 5:
Diagrams to determine the counter term.
(a), (b), (c), (d) are diagrams for the first order vertex, first order self-energy, second order vertex, and second order self-energy, respectively.

The second order vertex is contributed by diagrams in
fig.

5
(c).
The last two diagrams cancel each other because
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(1)}(\omega)=0
.
Then
\Lambda^{(2)}
should cancel the first two diagrams at zero frequency.
We do not attempt to calculate
\Lambda^{(2)}
in this work.

We also do not attempt to calculate the full second order self-energy
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(2)}
.
Instead, we only show how to calculate its linear frequency-dependence,
i.e.
,
\partial_{\omega}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(2)}
.
The condition
\partial_{\omega}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(2)}=0
(
eq.

97
) will determine the counter term
\lambda_{0}^{(2)}
.

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(2)}
is contributed by the diagrams shown in
fig.

5
(d).
The last two diagrams cancel each other because
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(1)}(\omega)=0
according to the second paragraph above.
The third diagram is contributed by
\Lambda^{(2)}
, which by definition is
\omega
-independent (
eq.

94
) and hence does not contribute to
\partial_{\omega}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(2)}
.
Thus, we only need to consider the first two diagrams, dubbed as
\Sigma^{(2,1)}
and
\Sigma^{(2,2)}
, respectively.
The first diagram gives the correction

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(2,1)}(1)=

\displaystyle-\left\langle f(1)\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{T^{2}}{4^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2345\\
5^{\prime}4^{\prime}3^{\prime}2^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\delta_{\omega_{2}+\omega_{3},\omega_{4}+\omega_{5}}\delta_{\omega_{5}^{\prime}+\omega_{4}^{\prime},\omega_{3}^{\prime}+\omega_{2}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(23;45)\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(5^{\prime}4^{\prime};3^{\prime}2^{\prime})f^{\dagger}(2)f^{\dagger}(3)f(4)f(5)f^{\dagger}(5^{\prime})f^{\dagger}(4^{\prime})f(3^{\prime})f(2^{\prime})\right)f^{\dagger}(1)\right\rangle_{0C}

(215)

Applying Wick’s theorem, there is

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(2,1)}(1)

\displaystyle=-2^{4}\frac{1}{2}\frac{T^{2}}{4^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2345\\
5^{\prime}4^{\prime}3^{\prime}2^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\delta_{\omega_{2}+\omega_{3},\omega_{4}+\omega_{5}}\delta_{\omega_{5}^{\prime}+\omega_{4}^{\prime},\omega_{3}^{\prime}+\omega_{2}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(23;45)\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}(5^{\prime}4^{\prime};3^{\prime}2^{\prime})

\displaystyle\times\hbox to5.97pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
